# Detailed change history

Every change, newest first, with the numbers that decided it. **This is the regression
suite.** This project has repeatedly been re-broken by reasoning about the code instead of
measuring it, so each entry records what was measured before and after — those figures are
how you tell whether a later change has quietly undone an earlier one.

Read this when you are about to change something and want to know what it cost to get
right. For *what shipped when*, see [`../CHANGELOG.md`](../CHANGELOG.md).

Entries predate the release-note split of 2026-08-22 and are not grouped by version: many
landed on the same day, and the useful axis here is the measurement, not the tag.

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| 2026-09-11 | **A tree is gated once: a green full suite run stamps the tree it measured, and the hook and `release.ps1` skip the suite for a stamped tree (github#93, decisions/0013).** Filed while cutting 2.4.0 as "the suite runs three times per release". Measured first: one full run under the `suite` lock is **587 s** wall — 8 s of builds, **133 s** of four parallel Chromes over 60 checks per fixture, **446 s** of the one-Chrome serial lane over 34 frame-sensitive checks per fixture (pointer-driven 248 s + frame-reading 183 s summed across fixtures); the static gates ahead of it total **10.5 s** (lint 6.3 s). Then the count: the PR into `main` is **not** a run — its one required check took **4 s** on #95; `release.ps1`'s `git push origin HEAD` after a website merge runs **nothing** — an up-to-date push hands the hook **zero ref lines** (tested against a bare remote), and a tag push is never gated; the two `develop` pushes that night landed **21 s and 29 s** after their merge commits, so both were `SKIP_SMOKE`'d by hand. The real runs were the dry run on `release/2.4.0` and `release.ps1`'s own. The premise held exactly: the ruleset on `main` requires a PR with no bypass actors, and the merge commits for 2.3.0, 2.4.0 and 2.4.1 each have a tree **byte-identical** to the `develop` tip they merged. So `scripts/suite-stamp.mjs` records `{tree, fixtures[{name,digest,day,pinned}], at, checks}` under `suite-passed/` in the git common dir after a full green default run of a clean tree (partial runs and dirty trees never stamp — `--only` ends with `not stamping this run`), the hook checks every pushed commit's tree and skips with the stamp named, `release.ps1` checks `HEAD` and gains `-ForceSuite`, and both take the `suite` lock around a real run and release it on every exit (neither did before; four sessions contended for it in 18 minutes that night). Self-test: hit on the same tree from a new commit and from a `--no-ff` merge, miss on a changed tree, on a regenerated fixture, on an unpinned fixture older than 7 days; a dirty tree refuses to record. The serial lane's 446 s is github#101. |
| 2026-09-10 | **One lock directory now covers every sister project, not just this repo (github#92).** A per-repo lock directory (`vault-graph-locks`) protected nothing across projects sharing the same machine's one Chrome and one screen: Vault Graph's suite and Vault Shelf's suite each held a lock the other could not see, and ran together anyway. `scripts/lock.mjs`'s `ROOT` is `obsidian-vault-locks` in the OS temp dir now, shared by every worktree of every sister project; a run that took its lock under the old per-repo root is still honoured until it ages out (`LEGACY_ROOTS`, checked *before* the new directory is claimed, since claiming it first would let a run start on top of a legacy holder it never saw). Made directly on `main` rather than through `develop` -- caught only because it was never pushed, discovered while reconciling `main` for the 2.4.0 release. Carried forward rather than discarded; the fix is real and dated today, it just took the wrong path in. |
| 2026-09-10 | **Two smoke checks read the walk mid-flight and called it rest, and one had a real source bug behind it (github#78, design/0006).** Rehearsing 2.4.0's release dry run, "legend count bars scale to the largest visible folder" and "hovering a note ramps in and releases at zero" failed intermittently -- roughly 1 run in 6-8, reproducing fully isolated with nothing else running concurrently, which ruled out shard contention as the cause. Both were reading a **frozen** page: the count-bars check polled `getComputedStyle(...).getPropertyValue('--vg-share')` twice 150ms apart and called it settled on two equal reads, and the hover check read `__vg.hoverT` after a fixed `sleep(400)` -- both false-positive whenever the page's own `requestAnimationFrame` loop simply had not been scheduled between samples, which a frame-driven ramp can do under real machine timing jitter without being done. Fixed by polling `__vg.demo.busy()` (`play || cascadeRun || anim || hoverRaf || hlRaf`) to actual completion instead, the same primitive the suite's own top-level `settle(p)` already used correctly. **The count-bars check also caught a real product bug once it could see the true end state**: `cascade()`'s 400ms stall watchdog (`STALL_MS`) calls `settle()` directly when the raf loop has not ticked in time, and that path skipped `barWalkEnd()` -- only the ordinary convergence branch called it -- so a watchdog-triggered settle left the legend's bars painted at whatever share the last tick reached, a real stuck bar on any machine that stalls a frame, not only a test artifact. Diagnosed by reading `__vg.lastCascade()` at the moment of failure: `exit: "settle() called from outside the loop"` with `ins:0, outs:4358` mid-animation, confirming the watchdog fired while the disc's own fade was still legitimately running. `barWalkEnd()` moved inside `settle()` itself (idempotent, so the convergence branch's own explicit call became redundant and was removed) so every path that ends a cascade lands the bars. Verified: 20/20 clean runs on both checks after the fix, was ~1-in-6-8 before. **A `--jobs 1` (fully serial) default was tried and reverted.** The theory was that the 4-lane parallel shard was racing itself; measured, it was not -- both fixes above hold at any `--jobs` value, isolated or not. Forcing serial by default was actively worse: `GRID` (window tiling) defaults to `JOBS > 1`, so `--jobs 1` opens the browser at a different window size, and "focus web stays above dim notes" -- a pixel sample of the focus halo -- failed **6/6** runs at that size, completely alone, against **0/6** at the `--jobs 4` size; isolated further, it failed identically whether it was the only check selected or one of several, ruling out state carried over from an adjacent check in the same shard. Left at the original default of 4. |
| 2026-09-10 | **The live refresh is filmed in Obsidian itself, and the take is measured (github#72, design/0014).** `scripts/record-live.mjs`: demo vault, built plugin, throwaway profile, Obsidian on the right monitor, the graph in one tab, a note typed in a second, the graph tab clicked back. Twice, from the log of the take that shipped: the outer note (into the 407-note Meeting Notes folder, 100 % of the disc radius) was on the disc **1,366 ms after the tab click** and the cascade was at rest after **3,859 ms**; the inner one (Weekly Reviews, 41 % of the radius) after **1,450 ms / 3,970 ms** -- the 500 ms wake plus the 1 s debounce, then the two-second cascade. Three things the first takes got wrong, each caught by a frame rather than by reasoning: (1) a settle that watched only dot positions declared the disc at rest while the intro was still sweeping the date range, because the sweep moves no dot -- the rest signal now also covers the range end, the page text and any notice; (2) Obsidian came up in this machine's language, so the clip sets `localStorage.language` to English and reloads before anything is filmed; (3) both hovers missed, twice, with the pointer visibly on the dot: `SetCursorPos` moves the cursor but injects no input, so the page never saw a mousemove -- every move is now followed by two 1 px `mouse_event` nudges, and the harness reads the tooltip back instead of assuming (hit first try on both, no nudge needed). The page take (`record-demo.ps1 -Act live`, 11.9 s) is kept as a second clip on the feature page. |
| 2026-09-09 | **The storyboard has a `live` act, so the follow-the-vault cascade can be filmed (github#72, design/0014).** The standalone page cannot watch a vault, so a `live` beat hands `applyData()` the build in hand plus one note -- `Untitled`, dated the newest day the disc holds, linked once to its folder's shallowest best-connected note -- filed in the biggest folder on the ring the beat names; an `arrival` target then finds that dot for a hover. Nine beats: outer, settle, hover, park, inner, settle, hover, park. Driven on a 524-note mirror of the real vault: the outer note went to the 251-note folder and the inner one to a 75-note folder, each cascade converged in 2.3 s, both hovers hit the arrival, 526 notes on the disc afterwards and `stats.nodes` agreeing. Left out of the full run: it adds two notes every act after it would have to know about. `docs/features/live.md` carries the clip commands; the clip itself is not yet recorded. |
| 2026-09-09 | **A live rebuild ran behind a hidden leaf, so nobody saw it (github#72, design/0014).** Driven in a real Obsidian on a throwaway copy of the demo fixture: open the graph, switch to another note, write one, switch back. **The note was taken in 1,315 ms after the write and the cascade finished while the leaf was hidden -- the disc moved on 20 sampled frames WHILE HIDDEN and 0 after switching back**, so the reader returned to a disc that had silently changed. That is the opposite of the feature's purpose. A rebuild is now held while `containerEl.offsetParent` is null, which is exactly while Obsidian has the leaf at `display:none` (measured false on 83 of 83 hidden samples); dirty paths keep accumulating and the whole burst lands as one cascade on return. **After: 0 samples moved while hidden, 20 after switching back, twice in a row.** The wake is a 500 ms poll that runs only while a rebuild is waiting, and that is measured rather than preferred: `active-leaf-change` and `layout-change` were tried first and **switching away fired five of them while `revealLeaf` fired none**, so a wake hung on those events never arrived; an earlier variant that tested visibility inside the handler also failed, because both events fire before Obsidian re-lays out the workspace and `offsetParent` is still null at that moment. New check: `obsidian-smoke.mjs --only live`. Note for whoever writes the next one -- __vg's demo/debug surface (`demo.busy`, `lastCascade`, `alpha`) is STRIPPED from the plugin build, so the first version of this check measured nothing and reported 'cascade none'; motion in a plugin build is read as a checksum over node positions sampled over time, and the outcome from `view.lastLive`. |
| 2026-09-09 | **A tag message was losing every markdown heading, and had been for three releases (github#47).** `git tag -F` defaults to `--cleanup=strip`, which treats a line starting with `#` as a comment and drops it — and the tag message is the CHANGELOG section, so the `## <version>` heading and every `###` section heading went with it. Measured on the tags themselves: **2.0.0, 2.1.0 and 2.2.0 each carry 0 heading lines**; 2.3.0's source section has **8**. `git show <tag>` is meant to tell the same story as the Release page and had been telling a flattened one. Found by checking the bytes of the tag before pushing it rather than trusting `git tag` to write what it was given — the console also renders the em dashes as `?`, which is a terminal codepage and not a defect, so the check that mattered was decoding the tag object as UTF-8 (19 em dashes, 0 mojibake). `release.ps1` passes `--cleanup=verbatim` now; 2.3.0's tag was re-cut before it was pushed and carries all 8 headings, and the three older tags keep the defect because a published tag is not edited. |
| 2026-09-09 | **The hero take works again: an absolute drag now aims at its destination instead of offsetting the press point (github#78's release).** The whole-storyboard take had died at the first `pin` beat every time for a day — `dragged 371 but it is not pinned afterward`, always at ~32.6 s — while recording that same act alone worked, which read like state the preceding act left behind. It was `demo.mjs`'s own arithmetic: `dx,dy` was measured from where the target was first resolved, then the press point was corrected for drift and the **stale delta applied to the corrected point**, so the drop landed short by exactly the drift. Back to back after `hoptrail`'s camera flight there was drift; with the few seconds' pause you get from recording one act in isolation there was none, which is why it looked like state. An absolute `to:` destination is re-resolved and aimed at now; a relative `drag: [dx, dy]` keeps its delta, because there the delta is the intent. **Not caused by the count bars:** swapping `src/page.js` and `src/page.css` to develop reproduced it identically, same note, same timestamp. Measured: the take went from dying at **32.6 s** to a complete **162 s** run, zero missed beats, and `assets/demo.webp` is 12.51 MB against 12.53 MB. Two lessons: a harness that passes in isolation and fails in sequence is suspect **in the harness**, and a correction applied to one half of a computed pair is worse than correcting neither, because it looks careful. |
| 2026-09-09 | **Every feature clip re-recorded so the gallery shows the count bars (github#78).** The legend is on screen in all sixteen acts, so all sixteen clips were stale the moment the bars landed. Re-recorded from the same storyboard on the right monitor, under the `record` lock: `folders` 29.9 s / 4.04 MB is the one that actually shows the walk (its `only` beat sends every other bar down to nothing while the soloed one fills its row). Two findings worth keeping. **`mobile`'s documented command silently upscaled it**: `make-hero.ps1` defaults to 960 px and the mobile take is 406 px wide, so following `docs/features/mobile.md` as written produced 2.67 MB of upscale against 1.09 MB at native width — the doc now carries `-Width 406` and says why. **The hero take (`assets/demo.webp`) could not be re-recorded**: the full storyboard dies at the first `pin` beat with `dragged 371 but it is not pinned afterward — the drop missed the hub`, deterministically, at 32.6 s of a ~32.5 s drive. Not caused by this branch: swapping `src/page.js` and `src/page.css` to develop and running the same pipeline fails identically, same note, same timestamp. The `pin` act alone records fine, so it is state the preceding `hoptrail` act leaves behind — which already ends with a `reset` beat and a settle, so the obvious cause is not it. Left for its own issue; the hero clip stays at its 2026-09-08 recording rather than shipping a truncated take. |
| 2026-09-09 | **The shrinking bar declared itself and painted nothing; every row now carries its own bar colour (github#78, design/0006).** Caught while capturing the review filmstrip: the shrink measured correctly in CSS — class `lg bar bar-out`, `--vg-share: 74.961%`, `background-size: max(0px, 74.961%) 2px` — and the screen was blank. The colour was emitted only on rows that already had a bar, so a row that **gained** one mid-walk (from `paintBars` adding the class) had `--vg-bar` empty and `background-image: none`. Fixed by emitting `--vg-bar` on every legend row: the class and the width live in two places, so the colour has to live in the one place always rendered. **The check that missed it read CSS, which is the third time in this issue that reading CSS or the wrong row passed a real bug** — it asserted the class, the share and the monotone descent, all correct. It now measures ink from a real screenshot, comparing the mean colour **inside** the bar against the mean **beyond its end** in the same pixel rows. Inside-vs-beyond rather than strip-above-vs-strip-below because the row separator spans the full width and cancels out of a vertical comparison — the first cut of the instrument passed the mutation for exactly that reason, and a second cut read 0 at rest because a 100% bar leaves no background to reference. Measured: **175 on the demo and the 10k, 119 on the shape vault**; with the mutation restored the reading is **0** and the check fails with `the shrinking bar was DECLARED but not painted (ink 0)`. A shrinking row sits at `opacity: .38`, so its bar is fainter than at rest — which is correct, the folder is leaving — and only a painted measurement distinguishes faint from absent. Directed suite **16/16 on each of the three fixtures**. |
| 2026-09-09 | **A bar that loses its folder now shrinks over the cascade instead of blinking out (github#78, design/0006).** Reported straight after the walk landed: `only` one folder and the others went to zero in a single frame while the disc took 1600ms to re-pack. The cause is that a hidden folder's resting share is 0, so the render dropped the `bar` class and the bar left the screen before the walk could touch it. Three changes together: **`paintBars` owns the class** — it queries `.lg[data-g]` rather than `.lg.bar[data-g]` and adds or removes `bar` as the painted width crosses zero; a **rebuild mid-cascade keeps the class** while `shown` is still above zero (`share || shown`); and the **4px presence floor is lifted** by a `.bar-out` class on a row walking down to nothing, via `max(var(--vg-floor, 4px), var(--vg-share))` — without it the shrink ends in a 4px stub that blinks out anyway, which is the same defect one step later. The floor override is a class, not an inline style, because the Obsidian lint rule forbids a static `setProperty` value. Measured on `only`: demo `05 - Meeting Notes` 100.0% to gone through **23 widths** and `14 - Reading List` 0.246% to gone through 23, leaving 1 bar and restoring 17 on All; the 10k 17 and 15 widths; the shape vault 25 and 24, restoring 6. The new check asserts the descent is **monotone** — never a step back up — because a walk started from the wrong endpoint bounces, and a bounce reads as a glitch rather than a mistake. Directed suite **16/16 on each of the three fixtures**. |
| 2026-09-09 | **The count bars now walk on the cascade's own clock (github#78, design/0006).** A bar's width is a fact about the layout, so when the layout walks the bar walks with it: `barWalkStart()` where the cascade loop is armed, `barWalkTick(ease)` beside the note interpolation off the same `pr = frame / span` and `ease = pr * pr * (3 - 2 * pr)`, and `barWalkEnd()` at the `converged` exit. **A CSS transition was the obvious alternative and is the wrong one** — it runs on its own clock and lands when its own duration expires, which puts a second source of truth next to the cascade's and breaks the animation.md core invariant. The landing is **by assignment, not by convergence**: `barWalkEnd()` paints `barNow` exactly rather than trusting the final lerp. Three maps, mirroring `colorShown`: `barPrev`/`barNow` are the endpoints recorded per render, `barShown` is what is on screen and is `null` at rest so the resting value stays authoritative, and it is read only while `cascadeRun` is live so a stuck walk cannot be observed. Because a click rebuilds the legend, the row's **class** comes from the resting share and its **width** from `barShown` — one value for both gives either a snap or a bar that outlives its folder. Measured, hiding the largest folder and watching the runner-up fill its row: demo `01 - Projects` **49.261% to 100.000%** through 23 distinct values, 10k `02 - Areas` **35.291% to 100.000%** through 24, shape `notes` **13.550% to 100.000%** through 23, each restored exactly on the un-hide. A separate harness sampled the share and a moving note's radius on the same ticks (radius 3328, 3405, 3302, 3252, 3354) and found the settled frame **identical to a fresh render on all 18 rows**. **The animation broke the existing bar checks, and that is the lesson worth keeping:** `count bars` read **63.807%** where it expected 100% — a `sleep(700)` racing a 1600ms cascade. Every fixed sleep after a state change is now `settleBars()`, which polls until no `--vg-share` moves; a time-based wait against an animation is a false failure waiting for a slow machine. New permanent check, `the count bars walk on the cascade's clock and land on the resting layout`, asserts at least two values strictly between start and target, a landing within 0.01 of 100%, and exact restoration. Directed suite **15/15 on each of the three fixtures**, the disc's own `the last frame of a cascade is the resting layout` included (demo 122f/1401 notes and 128f/1120 notes, dr 0 dtan 0 dot 0%). |
| 2026-09-09 | **The bar rule needed an id to survive Obsidian, and only the user's own running window could show it (github#78, design/0006).** "Works in Chrome, not in Obsidian" three rounds running. `.vault-graph .lg.bar` and `.vault-graph .lg:hover` have **identical specificity (0,3,0)**, so order decides — and inside Obsidian order is not the page's to choose. **Measured by attaching to the live window on a debug port:** `document.styleSheets` held **two** inline sheets for the page, #8 with 224 rules and the current CSS, and #9 with 241 rules — a stale copy carrying `.vault-graph .lg:hover { background: var(--surface-2); }`. The **shorthand** resets every background longhand and #9 came last. On one row, rest vs hover: `background-image` `linear-gradient(...)` → **`none`**, `background-size` `max(4px, 0.405%) 2px` → `auto`, `background-repeat` `no-repeat` → `repeat`, `background-position` `2px 100%` → `0% 0%`. Painted: **4px at rest, 0px hovering**. The selector is `.vault-graph #vg-legend .lg.bar` now — **(1,3,0), for specificity and not for scoping** — so it beats any `.lg:hover` a host or a stale sheet can produce without `!important`. Re-measured on the same row in the same live window after the fix: **4px and 4px**, `hovered=true`. **Both harnesses were structurally blind to this,** which is the lesson: `smoke.mjs` drives the standalone page where nothing else styles `.lg`, and `obsidian-smoke.mjs` builds a throwaway vault with a fresh profile, so it has no stale plugin stylesheet and cheerfully reported `image=gradient` under a real hover while the user's window was broken. A green harness on a synthetic host is not evidence about a real one. The check now asserts the bar rule's selector still contains an id and prints it. Directed suite **12/12 on each of the three fixtures**. |
| 2026-09-08 | **The bar now counts what is VISIBLE, and the page stops the host painting over it (github#78, design/0006).** Two reports in one: the highlight fix still did not work in Obsidian, and the bar should read the current visible note count rather than the total. **The Obsidian half was my own earlier mistake, recorded because the mistake is the lesson:** `.lg` is a `<button>`, Obsidian gives every button an inset shadow (white 9% at rest, 0.5px blur, **0.5px spread**), spread wraps all four edges including the bottom 2px strip the bar occupies, and inset shadows paint over a background image. I had tested exactly that hypothesis and cleared it — on the **100% bar**, where a 1.5px edge haze is invisible, getting 217px in all four states. On a **4px** bar it is the entire mark. **Measuring the widest bar to clear a defect that only shows on the thinnest one proves nothing**, and that is the second time in this issue that reading CSS or the wrong row passed a real bug. `.lg` now sets `box-shadow: none`, measured inside real Obsidian as `shadow=none` at rest and on hover, and `obsidian-smoke.mjs --only "hover"` **asserts** it so the host can never own the bar's visibility again. **The visible-count half is a model change:** `barShare` returns 0 for a hidden folder, so a closed eye means no bar rather than a clamped one, and the folder is already out of the basis. Measured on the demo: at rest 17 of 18 barred with a basis of 406; hide the largest and 16 remain with the hidden row drawing nothing and `01 - Projects` promoted to 100% at a basis of 200; **`only` one folder leaves exactly 1 barred row at 100%**; All restores 17. **The clamp this replaced was hiding an absurdity** — with the largest folder hidden its own row declared `max(4px, 203%)`, 203% of a basis it was no longer part of, which is exactly what the mutation now surfaces: restoring the old behaviour fails with `hidden 05 - Meeting Notes still draws a bar (max(4px, 203%) 2px)`. The check gained the `only` assertion and a hidden-row assertion alongside the rescale one. Directed suite **12/12 on each of the three fixtures**. |
| 2026-09-08 | **Highlighting a legend row erased the count bar on the smallest folders; the bar starts 2px in now and the floor is 4px (github#78, design/0006).** Reported twice from the plugin — first as hover, then "highlight in the grouping navigation removes the bar" — and the second report is what found it. **A background layer loses to anything painted above it, and two row states paint over the LEADING edge, which is exactly where a bar starts:** `:hover` turns the row's `1px solid transparent` border visible and its antialiasing costs **1px**, and `[data-hl="on"]` draws its leading channel as `box-shadow: inset 2px 0 0 0 var(--accent)` — **inset shadows paint over a background image** — costing **2px**. Measured on the demo at the old 3px floor: highlighting took floored rows from 3px to **0px**, and wide bars from 107 to 104. **Fixed with `background-position: 2px 100%`**, which starts the bar past the accent band, **plus a 4px floor** so the worst state still paints 3px. After: floored rows read **4px at rest, 4px hovering, 3px highlighted**; `01 - Projects` 107/107/106; `05 - Meeting Notes` 215/214/214 — every row keeps a mark in every state. **`background-origin: content-box` was tried first and is wrong:** it does move both overlays outside the track, but it lifts the bar out of the padding strip into the content row where the `only` chip lives, and that opaque chip punched the full bar from 205px to **152px on hover**. The bar belongs below the content, where no grid item can reach it. **Three traps the new pixel check hit before it was right,** each of which had it passing or throwing for the wrong reason: `p.j` wraps its expression in `JSON.stringify` and returns `undefined` for an async IIFE (it reported `undefinedpx`); **a click rebuilds the legend**, so a marker attribute set before the click is gone after it, and rows must be addressed by `data-g`; and it passed alone but failed in sequence because earlier checks open the whole tree and the thinnest row scrolls below the fold, where a clip captures nothing and reads as 0px. **And one guard against a false PASS:** a bar whose hue is the accent's cannot be distinguished from the highlight fill and reads **high** — 213px on the shape vault's `(vault root)` — so that single reading is dropped with an explicit note rather than trusted. The check now asserts the weakest of rest/hover/highlight is at least 3px. Directed suite **12/12 on each of the three fixtures**. |
| 2026-09-08 | **Hovering a legend row made the smallest folders' count bars vanish, so the floor is 3px now, not 1px (github#78, design/0006).** Reported from the plugin. `.lg:hover` turns the row's `1px solid transparent` border into a visible one, and that border's antialiasing costs the bar its leftmost pixel — which is nothing to a wide bar and everything to a floored one. **Measured on the demo, painting each row and counting bar-coloured pixels: at the 1px floor three of eighteen rows lost their bar entirely on hover** — `00 - Inbox`, `13 - Someday Maybe`, `14 - Reading List`, every row at or under 0.74% of the basis. Sampled in colour on `00 - Inbox`: at rest one pixel is the full `rgb(217, 89, 38)` at contrast **4.83** against the sidebar; hovering, the brightest is `rgb(140, 67, 37)`, a half blend at contrast **2.21**. Every wider bar loses a pixel too and nobody can tell — 217→215, 107→106, 76→75. **The floor was swept rather than guessed:** 1px paints 0 under hover, 2px paints 1 (a single blended pixel), 3px paints 2 and holds a solid mark, 4px paints 3. **3px is the smallest floor that survives the pointer**, so that is the number; the review's 1px was pinned before this was known. **The first explanation was wrong and was measured away rather than shipped:** `.lg` is a `<button>` and the page never resets `box-shadow`, so Obsidian's own button shadow applies and roughly doubles on hover (white 9%→16%, blur 0.5→1px, spread 0.5px), and inset shadows paint over a background image — but applying Obsidian's exact rest and hover shadows to the standalone page left the painted bar at **217px in all four states**. Two checks landed. `smoke.mjs --only "thinnest count bar"` hovers the thinnest barred row with a real pointer and counts PAINTED pixels rather than reading CSS, which is the gap the old hover assertion had — it read `backgroundImage` and `backgroundSize`, both of which survive perfectly while the mark disappears. It fails at the 1px floor (`1px painted at rest, 0px hovering`). `obsidian-smoke.mjs --only "hover"` does the CSS half inside a real Obsidian and reports the host box-shadow it finds, so nobody rediscovers that Obsidian styles this button. **A trap worth keeping:** `p.j` wraps its expression in `JSON.stringify` and so returns `undefined` for an async IIFE — anything decoding a screenshot through a canvas must use `p.eval`, which awaits. The first cut of the pixel check reported `undefinedpx painted`. |

So the two folds buy different things and the arithmetic says why: `matrixFromCamera` scales by `min(width, height)`, and at a landscape window the constrained axis is the height the band is eating — **the band buys 22% of dot radius and halves the sub-2-px population, the sidebar buys framing and not one pixel of radius.** The same effect `design/0013` measured on a phone, where the band cost nothing at all (1.38 px median with it and without). **The camera is not touched and needs no touch**: ratio 1.08 at 0.5,0.5 through all five states, because the camera ratio is dimension-independent and `renderer.render()` → `resize()` re-frames the disc in the new box on its own. Auto-fitting here would have fought the shipped *a manually moved camera is left alone by a visibility toggle*. **The resting desktop layout does not move**, which is the other half of the claim and was measured rather than assumed: `mobile-check --device desktop` reports sidebar 288x1000, stage 1312x1000, heat 1312x230, canvas 1312x770 and radius 0.89/2.19/4.06 both before and after the change, identical. **Two defects the phone's path was carrying into desktop, found by tracing rather than by a failure.** `clickStage` and `select(id)` both called `setSheet(false)` unguarded — correct for an overlay a tap should dismiss, and at desktop it means clicking bare disc or opening a note folds the sidebar away. Both are now gated on `narrow()`, and the new check asserts the round trip rather than the reasoning: a stage click and opening the highest-degree note leave `data-sheet` at `on`. **Panel state is now persisted, reversing `design/0013`'s "session state, nothing is persisted".** It goes through `decisions/0009`'s channel — `sheetOpen` / `onSheetOpen` and `bandOpen` / `onBandOpen` on the deps object, `localStorage` in `shell.html` and `data.json` in the plugin, and the page itself still stores nothing. Absence of the dep is what means *nobody has chosen yet*, so a first open decides from the width: a phone summons the legend, a desktop keeps it beside the disc. Persisting writes exactly when a call is not `quiet`, since the only quiet calls are the mount restoring what was stored. The accepted cost, recorded as a limit rather than discovered later: Obsidian's `data.json` is shared between desktop and mobile, so a desktop user who has toggled the sidebar back on gives their phone a sheet that is open at mount. New check *the panel toggles fold each panel away and give the space back* — 84 checks now, green on all three fixtures, 2.9s each: it pins its own 1280x900 viewport (the suite's window is a grid slot sized from the screen and `--jobs`, and every claim is a pixel gain), and reads canvas 992x670 → 1280x670 → 1280x900 = the root box exactly. **The cluster's placement took three tries and the third one animates rather than relocates.** It rides the disc box's top-left, one inset in, which is the corner folding actually moves — 288px off the left edge with the folder list, 230px off the top with the calendar. First attempt: the view's own top-left, on the reading that it should match `#vg-cam`'s 12px. Measured, `#vg-cam` sits 12px from the view's bottom-right because that corner of `#vg-canvas` *is* the view's, while `#vg-mob`'s 12px read as 300px and 242px in — but matching it puts the buttons on the folder list header, outside the disc, on top of the panel they fold, and it was rejected on sight. Second: the bottom-right beside `#vg-cam`, the only placement that holds still, and 1500px from the folder list it folds — rejected as no longer a handle. The geometry is why the shortlist was that short: folding moves the box's left and top edges only, so proximity and stability are exclusive. **Third and kept: leave it on the corner and animate the move.** `glidePanels()` measures the box, writes the attribute, measures again and plays the delta with `el.animate()` over 180ms. A CSS transition was tried first and cannot work — inside `#vg-canvas` the cluster's own left/top stay 12px and only the container moves, so there is nothing to interpolate; re-anchoring to the root with `calc()` over published edge variables was the next attempt and was dropped, since the animation needs no re-parenting and the phone stays exactly as design/0013 measured it. Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`: **0 animations, transform `none`**, the fold snapping, which is the sheet's own treatment. Worth knowing before anyone re-measures this and concludes it is broken: **the author's machine reports `reduce: true`**, so the glide does not play there, and an earlier reading of "the transition never fires" was that setting rather than the CSS. **And for the whole of this work the cluster's inset was the plugin's 44px, not 12px.** `plugin/styles.css` raises `--controls-inset` to 44px under Obsidian because the status bar floats over the bottom-right corner `#vg-cam` occupies (github#4); the new cluster inherited it and sat 44px into the disc's top-left, where nothing floats. Reproduced **4 times out of 5 takes** on 2026-09-08, the one success being the take whose clip ships. Isolated from this release's own work rather than assumed: shooting it again with `glidePanels()` forced to its early-out reproduces the miss identically, so the panel animation is not the cause. And both panels change the canvas box without changing the root's, so neither the engine's window listener nor the page's root observer fires: each toggle calls `refreshSizeScale()`, `placeLogo()` and an explicit `renderer.refresh()`. The detail card also became a foot sheet at 46%: with 48 linked notes it had filled the whole phone screen and the disc was gone behind it, and both control clusters moved to the top corners to clear it. The harness gained three fixes of its own while doing this -- it finds the shared fixture store from inside a worktree, resets the camera before aiming a tap (a fling plus a pinch can carry the target dot off the stage), and closes the card between its two tap paths, which had been selecting a neighbour through one of the card's own links. **And the adversarial review pass earned four fixes before any of this shipped, one of them the feature itself.** The first cut set `isMoving` on every single-finger move and then asked `isMoving` before it asked how far the finger had travelled; `isMoving` decays 100 ms after the last move, so a tap only survived if the finger held still longer than that after its own wobble. **Tap-to-select would have been a coin flip on real hardware while every number here read correct**, because the harness sent `touchStart` then `touchEnd` with no move in between and so could never enter that branch. Under the slop nothing happens at all now -- no pan, no inertia -- and `touchMoved` alone decides at release. Three gesture probes were added for the gap: a tap after a 5 px wobble **selects with the camera held**, a 45 px swipe **pans and selects nothing**, a two-finger tap **selects nothing with the camera held**; all three on every viewport. The same pass found that `touchstart` reset `touchMoved` mid-gesture, so a long pan plus a resting second finger emitted a tap on a note nobody aimed at (only a gesture starting from no fingers resets it now, and a tap must have been one finger throughout, tracked as `maxTouches`); that a double-tap was position-blind, so tapping note A then note B within 300 ms called `fit()` instead of selecting B (`TOUCH_DOUBLE_TAP_PX` 24 px); and that nothing cancelled an in-flight camera animation, so a finger could not arrest a fling for 200 ms and a tap during one picked against a camera mid-flight (`Camera.stopAnimation()`). Four page-side fixes came with it: the two mount-time panel calls were each running a full `renderer.refresh()`, which re-adds every node and edge at a measured 9.4-21 ms on the 10k fixture, so they now pass a `quiet` flag and a real toggle calls `render()` rather than `refresh()` -- **desktop stopped paying for a phone feature it cannot reach**; the closed sheet is `visibility: hidden` rather than merely translated away, since every control inside it stayed tabbable and focusing one scrolls an `overflow: hidden` root with no scrollbar to scroll back; selecting a note closes the sheet, which otherwise covered the card at a higher z-index; and the two panel buttons went to 44 px on a coarse pointer, being the only route to the legend and the search box. |

The removal took the focus management that existed only to serve the keys (`ROOT.tabIndex`, the card taking focus on every selection, the `.vault-graph:focus` rule) -- a card that grabs focus when you click a dot is its own small way of overruling the host, and with no key to receive it there was nothing left to argue for it. The keyboard cost is real and unmitigated: there is no key path back along the walk. | 2026-09-05 | **An arriving note's fade never reverses during a solo switch (github#67).** Reported right after github#66: switching from one soloed small folder to another made the arriving notes flicker. Reproduced on the mirror (solo a one-note folder, then a four-note one), sampling every ~25 ms: 3 of the 4 arriving notes reversed direction repeatedly — one read alpha **0.02 0.12 0.02 0.89 0.08 0.97 … 1 0.43 0.5 0.58 0.65 1 0.79 0.82 0.87 0.07 0.11 0.99**, and its radius followed (the radius rides the alpha). Identical before and after #66. Accessors on the arriving notes' alpha entries recorded every write with its caller: **121 of 127 came from the cascade's own frame loop, `step`** — one writer alternating between two schedules. The cause: the block that spreads a ramped group's fades across the whole cascade ("A RAMPED GROUP'S FADES FILL THE WHOLE CASCADE") sat **inside `step`**, running every frame, and for a show group it re-sorted the arriving notes by their **current** radius and handed the sorted delays back out in that order; the arriving notes are being walked, so two of them swap radial order between frames, swap delays, and each fade restarts from wherever the other's delay puts it. The hide branch re-sorted the leaving notes the same way. **Fixed by computing the schedule once, before the frame loop, from positions that do not move:** a hide ordered by `posSrc`, a show by `finalPos` (the destination layout the cascade already has). After: **0 reversals on all four arriving notes**, alphas monotone 0.01 → 1. New serial-lane check *an arriving note's fade never reverses during a solo switch*: solos the two smallest groups with ≥2 notes in turn and counts, per arriving note, drops of more than 0.02 after a rise. `animation.md` states the rule: a schedule is decided once, from numbers that stand still. |
| 2026-09-05 | **A dot never outgrows its resting size while a cascade walks (github#66).** Reported while reviewing the branch: soloing a small folder made the notes "grow a lot and overlap, then shrink again". Reproduced on a mirror of the reporting vault (~500 notes, soloing a four-note folder) with a per-frame CDP probe: biggest dot **6.8 → 36.0 px** at 1.6 s, **12 overlapping pairs at worst 34 px** at 1.0 s, then 9.1 px when the cascade landed and 13.5 px after the auto-fit zoom. Identical on develop and the branch — pre-existing. The demo vault's 200-note folder does not show it (dots grow monotonically to 14.8 px). The terms, per frame: the inner band's room walked 96 → 923 units while its pitch walked 191 → 573, so `room / pitch` rose 0.5 → 1.88 mid-walk; the ramp top rose 8.4 → 21.8; the notes still waiting to leave (about 35 fading at any moment, the rest at full alpha) were sized off that product. Their destination size was 9 px the whole time — `HUB_ROW0_FRAC`, the github#53 cap for a row-0 inner note, which only takes hold on the frame the survivors arrive. **Fixed as a per-note bound, not a new walk:** `roomOf()`, which already lays out both endpoint packings to measure their rooms, now also asks `dotPx()` what every note would be drawn at while resting in each, and `cascade()` keeps the larger of the two per note on `cascadeRun.sizeCap`; `dotPx()` holds a note to it for the run's duration, so nothing is made to shrink early and nothing balloons in the middle; the bound dies with the run at `settle()` and every cancel site. After, same probe: **6.8 → 9.1 → 9.1 px, 9 pairs at worst 9.6 px** (the leaving notes crossing as they fade); dense solos unchanged (peak = rest, 1.00x). New serial-lane check *a dot never outgrows its resting size while a cascade walks*: solos the smallest group with two or more notes and asserts the per-frame biggest full-alpha dot, in graph units, against 1.05x the larger resting size — **fails on the old build (1.61x over on the mirror's two-note folder), passes on the new**, and passes on all three fixtures with the peak exactly at the resting size (84.2 / 102.2 / 286.2 units). First attempt declared the cap with `var` inside an inner function, so the frame loop saw `undefined`, threw, and the cascade snapped — caught by the same probe (all 493 notes at 41 px, 975 overlaps, landed in 0.4 s) before it was committed. `animation.md` carries the rule. |

| 2026-09-05 | **`mountVaultGraph` returns a `destroy()`, and the plugin's teardown calls it (github#62).** Tearing a mount down was `api.renderer.kill()` plus an emptied container, which released the three WebGL contexts and nothing else: the mount stayed alive through the `mousemove` and `visibilitychange` listeners it had put on the document and the `ResizeObserver`s on its root and heatmap band, so every view close, popout/re-dock and Refresh in Obsidian retained a whole graph. Measured on the 10k fixture, six kill+remount cycles of the plugin's own sequence on the standalone build (`HeapProfiler.collectGarbage` twice before every read): heap **14.1 → 55.9 MB**, DOM nodes **632 → 4107**, JS listeners **140 → 926**, document `mousemove` listeners **2 → 8**, `visibilitychange` **1 → 7** — **+579 nodes, +131 listeners, +1 of each document listener, ~+7 MB per cycle**. Torn down mid-intro (`--quick`, the "three quick cycles" of the report), the killed mount was still `busy` at +400 ms and +1.2 s with its cascade at **159 → 182 frames**, converging at 207 ~2.4 s later — a cascade over the whole vault drawn to a dead renderer. Fixed with an `onDestroy` list in page.js: every site that registers something outside the root pushes its undo there (root RO, document mousemove — removed with the same capture flag it was added with — and visibilitychange, heatwrap RO, heat-slot RO, the context menu's three listeners), plus `dead` guards on the three resize callbacks, `cascade()` and the boot timer; `destroy()` cancels every frame and timer in flight (play, cascade, tween, hover, highlight, colour), runs the list in reverse, kills the renderer last (its `kill()` is idempotent, so the order is free), clears `window.__vg` if it is this mount's, and nulls the handle's `api`. The plugin's `teardown()` calls `handle.destroy()` and still empties its own container. A list rather than named fields because the sites are scattered across 15k lines and a forgotten field is the leak. After, same measurement through the new harness: heap **14.1 → 14.4 MB**, nodes **632 → 633**, listeners **140 → 140**, document listeners **2/1 → 2/1** over six cycles — the load baseline, held; mid-intro the old cascade stops at **155 frames, `busy` false at +400 ms**. | 2026-09-04 | **What the adversarial review of github#58 found, fixed.** A fresh-context reviewer over the whole diff, with the Sigma 3.0.2 source beside it, verified every gesture, camera, event-ordering and store behaviour the page and the suite depend on as identical, and found seven things around them. (1) **The exporter's output depended on the working directory**: esbuild labels each bundled module with a comment holding its path relative to the process cwd, so `build-graph.mjs` run from the vault (`refresh-graph.ps1`) and from the repo produced files differing from line 1248 on -- while its own comment claimed byte-for-byte determinism. `absWorkingDir: ROOT` fixes it; **measured: the same fixture built from two directories is now byte-identical**. esbuild's errors are also printed as messages now rather than lost behind `logLevel: "silent"`. (2) **The README still promised a zero-install generator** ("No `npm install`") that the release zip could no longer deliver, and esbuild sat in `devDependencies`, so the natural `npm ci --omit=dev` installed nothing. esbuild is a runtime dependency now (the lockfile moves it), and README, `releasing.md` and `make-package.mjs` say `npm ci --omit=dev` once. (3) **The store's Sigma-compat facet outlived the switch by one commit**; deleted, with the page.js header paragraph that still called the renderer Sigma's. (4) **`kill()` left the camera wired**: a tween cut short by closing the view -- `fit()`'s 380 ms flight, drag inertia -- would keep scheduling full renders on lost contexts, silently. The camera has a `kill()` now, the renderer calls it, and a `killed` flag short-circuits `refresh`, `render` and both schedulers. (5) The label rule follows the reducer's type (a string is a label, anything else is none) instead of widening to `unknown` for numbers no reducer can return. (6) Listeners are a Set, so a function registered twice on one event fires once where Node's EventEmitter fired twice -- nothing does that; recorded in 0012. (7) Stale text: the pre-push hook's network-failure message pointed at `src/vendor.mjs`; `install-spike.ps1` still copied the deleted bundles; three page.js comments described Sigma's quadtree and label grid as current. `getCanvases()` keeps `Record<string, …>` on purpose: page.js indexes it with a string list that includes a layer the engine no longer has. | 2026-09-04 | **`compact axis: a year's width tracks its own note count` was asserting on a foreshortened total, and failed on the dominant-folder fixture with the axis behaving correctly.** It compared the busiest year's drawn pixels against the quietest's, but the year holding the **incomplete final month** is deliberately narrower: github#51 gives that month only the share its elapsed days have earned (`lastFrac`), so the range handle cannot sit past the newest note. That deduction is a fraction of one month's width, which is bigger than the gap between two years of similar size once both sit at or near `yearRef`'s ceiling. **Measured on the fixture the day it was found:** span 2025-07-01 → 2026-09-04, so 2025 held 460 notes over 6 months and 2026 held 494 over 9; `yearRef` came to 494, giving 2025 a weight of 11.24 average-months against 2026's full ceiling of 12 -- **the right order**. Then 2026's September, 4 days into a 30-day month, gave back 0.87 of its 1.33-month share, leaving 10.84 against 11.24 and drawing **465px against 483px**. The axis and its ordering were both correct; the assertion was reading a foreshortened total as an allotted one. The check now sets aside the year holding the final month and compares the full years, reporting NOT ASSERTED with the reason when fewer than two remain. **Still asserts where it matters** -- demo `2025 (247 notes) 235px` vs `2016 (16) 38px`, 10k `2022 (1011) 96px` vs `2016 (311) 35px` -- and **verified it still catches its own failure mode**: pinning `yFrac` to a constant so note count stops mattering made both years draw 87px and failed both fixtures. Fixed with one line: `return (state.hovered && state.hovered !== state.selected) ? hoverT : 1;` -- hovering a *different*, unselected note is untouched (still ramps via `hoverT`), hovering nothing is untouched (still `1`), only hovering the selected note itself now stays pinned at `1` for the whole enter/leave cycle. Real folders now rank ahead of both pseudo-groups (previously behind), which reorders the automatic twelve-slot colour rotation too -- colour is positional, not identity-locked, so this is an accepted side effect, not a regression. **This is also the first time in the ticket a rank change moved the LAYOUT, not just the legend**: `order[state.dim]` drives wedge ANGULAR POSITION as well as display order, something a purely-additive change (an empty group costing nothing) had not exercised before -- confirmed by the golden-snapshot check failing on all three fixtures with radius unchanged and only angle differing (up to several degrees, hundreds of notes per fixture), a real and expected rearrangement rather than a bug. Verified via a fresh golden-snapshot regeneration (angle changed again, moving most of the way back toward the pre-reorder values) and a live check that `(unlinked)` alone moved while every real folder's `slotOf()` returned to what it was before any of today's reordering. **Re-read the original reopen comment closely and found a second ask this ticket had silently dropped when the scope moved to full membership**: "giving them a mixed color dot in the navigation ... possible from the right click color menu for the unlinked group only" describes COLOUR ONLY, independent of "make unlinked optional completely ... to have those notes just as part of their folders" (the membership/move ask, in the same comment, hedged with "maybe" and "completely" as the bigger of the two). The shipped `unlinkedByFolder` covers the second half; the first half -- a note that STAYS in the `(unlinked)` group but still takes its own folder's tint -- had no toggle. Restored as `unlinkedTintByFolder` (default off), the near-verbatim original colour-only implementation from earlier the same day (`nodeColor()`'s `groupOf(id) === UNLINKED && !unlinkedTintByFolder` gate, `buildUnlinkedTint()`/`unlinkedTintColors`, the conic-gradient `swatchFill`/`swatchTitle` pair) reinstated as an ADDITIONAL toggle rather than the replacement it briefly was -- only meaningful, and only offered in the right-click menu, while `unlinkedByFolder` is off (nothing left in the group to recolour once every unlinked note has joined its folder). Also added on the same re-read: the `(unlinked)` row's own count reads `(13)` rather than `13` while kept separate, since -- unlike every other row's count, which is exactly that folder's own total -- this one counts a population no folder's own total includes. Two new smoke checks (`"...tint toggle recolours notes without moving them"`, explicitly asserting the count is unchanged after the colour flips, and `"...count is parenthesised..."`) plus a new settings-panel id-drift check for the third `OPTION_ROWS` entry. Full suite green on all three fixtures after both fixes. **Given its own gallery entry and demo beat the same day**, at explicit request: `docs/features/unlinked.md` split out of `docs/features/colours.md` (which now just cross-links it) rather than staying a paragraph inside an existing page, matching how compactaxis and subfoldercolor earned their own entries before it. A new `act: "unlinked"` in `demoMode()` runs LAST of all, after "colours" -- both toggles here touch colour (the tint half) and membership (the join half moves notes between wedges), inheriting "runs last" from colours and adding a reason colours never had: nothing later needs a clean starting membership, because nothing runs after this. Two new `demoFind` kinds, `ctxbyfolder`/`ctxtint`, mirror `ctxvis` exactly; the right-click target itself needed no new kind at all, since `demoGroup("(unlinked)")`'s existing prefix match already resolves the literal pseudo-group name, and the row is now guaranteed present on every fixture (see the always-in-the-legend fix above) so the beat needs no vault-specific setup the way subfoldercolor's twisty-opening does. Verified beat-by-beat via `scripts/demo.mjs --act unlinked` (real CDP input, not `el.click()`) before recording for real -- all 18 beats resolved and landed clean on the first attempt. Recorded both the per-feature clip (`assets/features/unlinked.webp`) and a fresh full hero (`assets/demo.webp`, 102 beats now, up from 84) against the synthetic demo vault, never the real one -- `record-demo.ps1`'s own default. Full `node scripts/smoke.mjs` green on all three fixtures once more after the storyboard change, and `check-pii`/`check-scope`/`check-network` clean. |

Verified the premise first: built the demo and shape vaults twice each, 3.5 years apart in `--end` (the same technique `check-generator-determinism.mjs` uses, extended from folder counts to the actual computed layout) -- band and every note's `(x, y)` identical to the full float64, both vaults, both dates, so a snapshot taken today does not go stale on the fixture store's own weekly `--end` refresh. Hit, then fixed, the same class of bug github#21 already named: reading positions straight off `demo.busy() === false` (even with a bare `applyLayout(false)`, once or twice) left two consecutive measurements of the IDENTICAL build disagreeing by several graph units on 90%+ of the demo and 10k vaults' notes -- a still-running cascade's next animation frame landing after the snap, exactly what `.ai-context/invariants.md`'s github#21 section already recommends `__vg.relayout()` for. Switched both the writer and the check to `__vg.relayout()` (cancels the in-flight frame, clears `roomNow`/`cellNow`/`edgeNow`/`bandLock`/`geomLock`, rebuilds) -- repeated measurements became byte-for-byte identical, confirmed across multiple runs on all three vaults. Also switched the writer from an independently-generated private vault to the SAME shared `.fixtures/<name>-<digest>` store `resolveVaults()` uses, so the snapshot and the check are guaranteed to read identical underlying vault content rather than two separately-generated (if supposedly-identical) copies. Confirmed non-vacuous: temporarily changed `INNER_SCALE` 0.8 -> 0.75 and reran without regenerating the snapshot -- failed on all three vaults, naming the exact folder that flipped band on the demo vault (`04 - Daily Notes: outer -> inner`) and every moved note's id and delta on the other two; reverted immediately after. `.ai-context/invariants.md`'s "A vault's layout matches its golden snapshot" section carries the full account. |
| 2026-08-27 | **Row 0 of the inner band is capped against the hub's own radius, closing the dot-sizing half of github#35.** The hub-boundary-position half (`inHubHole`/`placeHubDrop`/`placeLogo` testing the wrong radius) shipped in 1.8.0; this is the other half, left open because a first attempt regressed. `placeCell` puts row 0 of the inner band at `r0 * INNER_SCALE` exactly -- the same circle the hub boundary is drawn at -- for every inner band, always; normally invisible since a healthy dot is a small fraction of that radius. Soloing a folder down to a single note that lands alone in the inner band collapses the band to that one row, so `spInner = thickI / rows` becomes the band's WHOLE thickness (measured: 573 units) instead of a normal row's slice, and `rampFor` sizes the dot off that pitch -- the ramp balloons, not the room/pitch shrink factor (measured `f = room/pit` at 1.02, barely above 1, on the reported repro; that was the first, wrong guess). A band-wide cap on `room` (the `sqrt(full/now)` density ratio `bandDensity()` already uses for row spacing) was tried and **reverted same day**: it fixed the reported case but broke `"filtered to the bone, the disc stays drawable"` on two other fixture vaults -- ordinary aggressive date filtering also produces sparse bands, unrelated to the hub, and a band-wide cap can't tell the two apart. Measured bisecting it: dominant-folder vault `range last 2.5%` diameter/step 0.07 against a 0.15 floor, 10k vault `range last 0.5%` at 0.12, both passing at 0.33 and 0.2+ with the cap reverted. **Fixed instead** with a new PER-NOTE cap scoped to row 0 of the inner band only (`HUB_ROW0_FRAC`, `src/page.js`), following the existing `edgeCap` pattern (a hard geometric bound computed once during placement, consumed alongside `edgeCap`'s own cap at the end of `dotPx`) rather than touching `room`/`sp`/the ramp -- it cannot affect any other row or band, so it cannot repeat the regression. `HUB_ROW0_FRAC = 0.08`: measured a healthy row-0 dot's own radius as a fraction of `r0 * INNER_SCALE * UNIT` on the demo vault at rest, 4.2% (36 of 851 units, the biggest dot in a 6-row inner band) -- doubled for margin. Verified live: soloing `01 - Projects` (the originally reported real-vault repro, 5 notes) no longer overflows the hub; on the demo vault, forcing a single note alone into the inner band (varies by folder and by the vault's own day-anchored generation, since note weight decides how far the uncapped size would have gone) now caps at exactly 68 of 851 units, 8.0%, matching `HUB_ROW0_FRAC` precisely, against 505 (59%) and 81 (9.5%) uncapped on two different folders that hit the same shape before this fix. `scripts/smoke.mjs`'s `"filtered to the bone"` stayed green throughout (unlike the reverted attempt), and a new check, `"a soloed hub-adjacent note stays inside the hub's own radius"`, tries every folder in turn and asserts the worst result stays under 15% -- the shape none of the three fixtures' resting state hit, which is how this shipped unnoticed the first time. |
| 2026-08-26 | **`settle()` now refreshes dot SIZE, not just position, closing github#21.** `dotPx()` -- called by the Sigma node reducer on every paint -- reads `bandOf().room`/`cellRoom`/`edgeCap` as plain persistent globals, and those are side effects of whichever `ringsLayout()` call ran most recently. For the ~90 frames of a cascade that was the frame loop's own calls, walking `roomNow`/`cellNow`/`edgeNow` from the source packing toward the destination's *endpoint capture* (`roomOf()` over a separately-built `staticPlan`) -- a different computation from the one `finalPos` (what `settle()` actually assigns for POSITION) came from. Once the loop stopped nothing ever re-derived the correct figure, so every dot rendered at whatever the last animated frame's walked room happened to be, until an unrelated relayout (a resize, a fit, the next toggle) silently corrected it by accident. Measured live on the demo vault: `05 - Meeting Notes / 2025-09-19 Vendor call` rendered at 26.5px right after a range-change cascade settled, 68.1px after nothing but a bare `__vg.ringsLayout()` call with no position change -- 157% off, position exactly right throughout (`dr`/`dtan` both 0). 211 of 213 sampled notes were off by more than 5%; a folder toggle showed the same defect smaller (152 of 689, worst 11.6%). Invisible to the existing "last frame of a cascade is the resting layout" check (`scripts/smoke.mjs`) because it compares the last ANIMATED frame against rest and both read the identical stale globals -- confirmed by reverting the fix and re-running that check, which still reported `dot 0%` clean. **Fixed** by having `settle()` call `ringsLayout()` again after `assignPositions`, discarding its position output (reusing it would reintroduce the "two plans disagree on seats" 1517-unit jump the adjacent comment already documents fixing once) and keeping only the room/cellRoom/edgeCap side effects. **Once was not enough**: room and position are a fixed point (same reason `finalPos` itself is computed twice, "TWICE, and the first one is thrown away"), and a single settle-time call still measures its margins against the stale room the frame loop left behind -- measured, one call alone left 58 of 213 notes over 5% (worst 39.1%), a second immediate call converged all of them to 0%, a third changed nothing. Also corrects the original github#21 writeup, which credited a settle-time refresh already shipped for folder toggles to branch `fix/small-folder-animation` -- that branch turned out to be an unrelated, stale (~30 commits behind, two reverts at its tip) wedge-seam-overlay experiment, and no such fix existed on `develop` before this entry. `.ai-context/invariants.md`'s "A settled dot is the SAME size a fresh relayout gives it, not just the same position" section carries the full account. |
| 2026-08-26 | **`walk()`'s directory listing is now sorted, closing github#32.** The generator-determinism check (github#33) ruled out the synthetic fixtures as the cause of a band-assignment discrepancy, but `walk()` in `src/build-graph.mjs` was still reading each directory with bare `readdirSync(dir)` — Node documents its order as filesystem-dependent, not a contract. That order flows straight through, unsorted, all the way to the layout: `walk()`'s return becomes `files`, which becomes `notes` in the same order (the per-file loop is a plain `for...of`, no sort in between), which becomes graph node insertion order (`graph.addNode` in file order), which becomes the group iteration order `balanceBands()` searches over in `src/page.js`. That search is exhaustive over which folders go inner vs outer and picks by `cm < bestCost` — strict less-than — so two candidate splits that tie on cost keep whichever the mask loop reached first, and the mask loop's order comes from `movable`, which comes from `names`, which comes from iterating `cells` in the same file-derived order. A tie that used to resolve on unspecified disk order could therefore pick a different inner/outer split for the exact same vault content between two otherwise-identical builds — the github#32 report (`04 - Daily Notes`, 4 sub-wedge cells, `inner: true, rows: 6` in one build and `inner: false, rows: 9` in another, same demo-vault directory, same command). Fixed with one line — `readdirSync(dir).sort()` — plus a comment at the call site tracing the chain, since nothing about `readdirSync`'s contract says this without it. New `scripts/check-build-order-determinism.mjs` guards it two ways, because the dynamic probe alone is not adversarial on every filesystem: on this NTFS checkout, `readdirSync` already comes back alphabetical even for directories populated in shuffled creation order, so a build with the `.sort()` removed still passes a same-vault-different-creation-order probe here — confirmed by reverting the fix locally and re-running the check, which still reported clean. So the check also does a static read of `walk()`'s own source (same idea as `check-scope.mjs`/`check-network.mjs`) asserting the `readdirSync(...).sort(` call is still there; reverting the fix and re-running with *that* half in place failed immediately with "walk() no longer sorts its readdirSync(dir) result". Wired into `.githooks/pre-push` alongside the generator-determinism check, no skip flag, same "cheap enough there's no reason to skip" bar (two tiny subprocess builds, no Chrome, well under a second). Verified against all three fixtures post-fix via `smoke.mjs --only lattice --only "row depth"`: demo vault inner 6 / outer 9 rows, 10k vault inner 15 / outer 23, dominant-folder vault inner 5 / outer 7 — all passing, band assignment unchanged from before the fix on every fixture that had a stable split to begin with. `.ai-context/invariants.md`'s "Build order does not affect the band split" section carries the full account. |
| 2026-08-26 | **Per-subfolder sub-wedge row-depth gate, unconditional (github#31).** `splitFor(g)` already refuses to split a folder into sub-wedges when its TOTAL note count can't fill the band's rows — an aggregate, folder-level test that says nothing about each individual resulting subfolder cell once a folder does split. Found by reviewing the demo vault after github#23 shipped: `04 - Daily Notes` (50 notes, 16 month subfolders) clears the folder-level bar as a whole, then 3 of its 4 resulting sub-cells sit below the outer band's own row depth — `"0:6/9*", "1:6/9*", "2:4/9*"` against a healthy `"3:34/9"` pooled tail — measured via `__vg.buildWedgePlan(false)`, while every other split folder on that vault (`01 - Projects`, `02 - Areas`, `03 - Resources`, `05 - Meeting Notes`, `06 - Zettelkasten`, `07 - Weekly Reviews`, `08 - Archive`, `15 - Courses`) had zero sparse cells. Each sparse cell independently triggers `placeCell`'s deliberate one-note-per-row spread (the right call for one genuinely sparse cell; visually odd repeated three times side by side in the same folder). New `subCellIndex(folder, sub, n, depth)` sits beside `subTintIndex`, not inside it: below `depth` (the caller's own already-computed `bandDepth[bk]`) a subfolder's notes redirect to the pooled-tail slot instead of its own rank; at or above, unchanged. Kept as a separate function specifically because `subTintIndex` also drives a subfolder's COLOUR (`buildSubShades`) — folding the row-depth check into it would have shifted a subfolder's tint whenever its geometric slot changed, an unwanted coupling this entry deliberately avoids. **`n` was, for one review cycle, a lookup this function made itself — `subCount`, a whole-vault tally built once at load and never refreshed — and an adversarial review caught it before it shipped.** Under a filter, a subfolder large in the whole vault but with almost nothing currently visible would still clear the threshold on its stale total, reproducing the exact sparse-cell defect this feature exists to remove — the identical bug class `buildWedgePlan`'s own comment already documents fixing for the FOLDER-level gate ("it also cannot see a filter"), just not carried over to this new per-subfolder one. Fixed the same way: a new `liveSub` map built in the same cascade-stable, filter-aware loop that already produces `liveG`/`liveN`, keyed by `folder + "/" + sub`; the call site passes `liveSub[...] || 0` in, and `subCellIndex` makes no count lookup of its own. New check *no non-tail split cell holds fewer notes than its band's row depth* drives `__vg.buildWedgePlan(false)` directly and is deliberately vault-agnostic — it asserts the general rule rather than hardcoding which folder is expected to be sparse, so an even vault with nothing to gate is a legitimate pass. Measured on the demo vault: 42 cells at rest, 24 non-tail split, none sparse (down from the 3 sparse cells measured pre-fix). A second new check, *the row-depth gate reads LIVE counts, not the whole-vault tally, under a filter*, drives `buildWedgePlan(true)` (not `(false)`, which every other check here uses) under a window sized to the fixture's own first 20% of history (`dateSpan.lo`/`hi`, not a fixed date) — a first cut reused the fixed two-day window the pin-and-filter check uses and reached zero split cells on any fixture, a check that cannot fail being no check at all, so it was widened until it wasn't. Confirmed non-vacuous by reintroducing the fixed bug: 2 of 3 fixtures caught it immediately (`01 - Projects` sub-cells reported at 1/2 and 5/6), the third (dominant-folder, only 6 live cells under this window) reports "vacuous on this fixture" honestly rather than a silent pass. Also added a read-only `__vg.subTailRank` (= `SUB_SLOTS - 1`) so both checks derive the pooled-tail rank instead of hardcoding it. **Shipped as a dev-only toggle first (`__vg.setSubwedgeGate`, off by default, no settings row) and the toggle is gone — the right call at the time, since this started as a hypothesis about a look rather than a confirmed fix, and the wrong call to still be carrying by the end of the same day's testing.** Live A/B rounds found the merge itself correct every time; what they also found, one round at a time, was three real bugs that existed only because the toggle needed to recompute and re-render LIVE, mid-session: a cascade race (flip the gate mid-intro, the plan updates correctly but the still-running intro cascade's own next frame overwrites the rendered positions with its stale target — measured **7** angular clusters instead of **1** at 200ms into the ~5.6s intro, fixed at every timing after `cancelCascade()`), a stuck timeline (the same interruption skipped the intro's own completion cleanup, `endSweep()`, leaving the date-strip handle and tooltip parked wherever the cut-off frame left them — fixed by also calling `stopPlay()`, which `cascade()` itself already calls at its own start and which `setSubwedgeGate` had not been), and an animation that only ever jumped (`applyLayout(false)` is an instant, unanimated snap — right for something meant to give console-fast feedback, wrong for something a person is watching happen; tried switching to `applyLayout(true)`'s 380ms tween, which fixed the jump). All three were real, all three were fixed, and all three stopped mattering the moment the gate became unconditional: with no runtime flip, the very first and only cascade of a normal session already computes the correct, merged layout from the start, so there is nothing left to race, no completion cleanup to skip, and no live re-render to animate. `relayout()` picked up the same `cancelCascade()`/`stopPlay()` treatment along the way (identical shape, identical latent gap, at explicit request) and was reverted back to its original form along with everything else once baking the gate in removed the need for any of it — a real, general finding about that function (and any future caller of the same reset-and-snap shape) that is now simply unexercised by this ticket rather than fixed by it; worth its own pass if `relayout()` is ever driven live again. `.ai-context/invariants.md`'s "A sub-wedge only earns its own slot if it can fill one" section carries the full account. |

| 2026-08-25 | **CORRECTION to the row below: the real-duration weighting it shipped was superseded the same day, by actually looking at it against the author's real vault rather than only the three synthetic fixtures.** The row below's design (a populated month keeps its own real ms duration, only literally-empty runs collapse) was an exact, provable no-op whenever nothing was empty — and that hid the actual defect, because "no month is literally empty" said nothing about how BUSY a month was. Measured live: **2023 (21 notes, thin enough to touch nearly every month) drew 172px against 2026 (399 notes, the vault's busiest year by far) at only 114px** — 2023 read as "every month populated" and kept full real-time weight throughout eleven months, while 2026 is only partway through its own calendar year. None of the three synthetic fixtures could have caught this: it needs a vault where note density and month-coverage disagree, which a generator tuned for "sparse vs dense YEARS" never produces on its own. **Replaced with note-count weighting, not calendar-time weighting.** Each YEAR gets a width between `YEAR_FLOOR_MS` (~1 month) and `YEAR_CEIL_MS` (~1 real year), scaled by its own note count against `yearRef` — the same p90-with-floor shape `nRef` already uses for bar height, reused here rather than invented fresh. After: 2026 draws 408px against 2023's 90px. A first pass also weighted the MONTHS within a year by their own note count; asked to make them equal-width instead during the same review — "month-to-month noise inside one year isn't a signal worth spending width on" — and that's what shipped: every month in a year gets an identical share of that year's own width. Two more defects surfaced only by looking, neither visible in any automated check: (1) `buildYears()`'s decision to skip alternating year labels was estimated from a vault-wide average (`totalWidth * 365.25days / totalSpan`), accurate only on a linear axis — once a cluster of sparse years can sit far closer together than that average while one busy year eats the rest of the strip, the average never notices the local squeeze, and year chips rendered overlapping while the check that should have skipped them read "plenty of room". Fixed to measure the real minimum gap between consecutive years' actual rendered positions and skip below ~28px (a chip's own approximate width) — confirmed clean at 260px and 1600px alike, where the old estimate had reported "fine" at both. (2) Driving the actual Obsidian plugin under CDP (`scripts/spike-check.mjs`, against a `make-mirror-vault.mjs` copy so no real vault content left the machine) measured year-chip buttons at **30px tall inside a 16px row** — `line-height`, `font-size` and `box-sizing` all read correctly as declared, meaning Obsidian's own theme CSS was contributing vertical padding the button's own padding shorthand did not survive against. Invisible in every one of this ticket's Chrome-only checks, since the standalone page has no competing `button` CSS to lose that fight to. Fixed with an explicit `height: 14px`, authoritative under `box-sizing: border-box` regardless of what else contributes padding. **A last request from the same review: a view-level toggle, since the plugin's gear opens Obsidian's own settings tab rather than an in-view panel, and "we cannot record the settings" from inside the graph itself otherwise.** A small icon button (`#vg-compact`, converging-chevron glyph) now sits left of the date-range fields at the top-right of the timeline row, mirroring `#vg-pan`'s corner-button shape exactly — a mode, `aria-pressed`, persists on click. Its addition reversed an earlier call in this same entry's history: `compactAxis`'s plugin-side dep was made read-only on the reasoning that "no in-view control exists on that host, only the settings-tab toggle" — true when it was written, false the moment this button shipped, so `onCompactAxis` was added back to `plugin/main.js`'s deps, matching `onPanEnabled` exactly. Verified against the real plugin under CDP a second time, not assumed from the standalone: rendered at 22px tall (no overflow), clicking flips `aria-pressed` and `__vg.compactAxis` and does not overlap the date fields (14px clearance). `.ai-context/invariants.md`'s "The date axis weighs years by note count" section carries the full account; this row exists so the row below reads as superseded rather than as what shipped. |
| 2026-08-25 | **The date axis compacts a sparse vault's empty runs, gated on `compactAxis` (default on, github#23).** On the author's own 457-note vault, 97 of 138 months (70%) hold zero notes and five whole years took 43.5% of the strip to represent seven notes. `buildDateSpan` now builds a segment table alongside the dense month list: a populated month keeps its own real duration as its weight, a maximal run of consecutive zero-note months collapses into one segment weighing one *average* month (30.436875 days) regardless of how many real months it spans. `ribbonX`/`ribbonMs` dispatch between the untouched linear formula and a new compact one that maps through this table; every existing caller (year chips, brush, window pill, tooltip, sweep) inherits it for free, since none of them reimplement the mapping — confirmed by reading every call site rather than assumed. `drawRibbon`'s bar layout, which used an index-based uniform pitch rather than `ribbonX` itself, was switched to iterate the same segment table so the bars agree with the control reading them; a collapsed run paints a dim marker rather than reusing the ordinary (invisible-when-empty) bar treatment, so the axis *shows* it is compacting rather than narrowing silently. **A flat weight of 1 per month was tried first and was wrong**, not merely imprecise: with zero gaps every segment is still exactly one month wide, which is a uniform axis, not the genuinely time-proportional one the linear formula computes — measured 0.56–4.45px drift on all three standard fixtures even with nothing to collapse. Weighting a month by its own real ms duration makes the sums telescope back to `dateSpan.lo`/`.hi` exactly, so "no gaps" now reduces to the linear formula byte-for-byte: **0px** delta across a dozen samples on all three fixtures, down from 0.56–4.45px. A hand-built 4-month gap (a scratch copy of a generated vault with three years of notes force-relocated out of a window) measured **8.2px compacted against 31.7px linear — 74% narrower**, confirming the collapse itself works. **None of the three standard fixtures contain a real empty-month run** — the 10k vault's own comment claims "one year holding none at all" but that describes the *real* vault it was modelled on, not the synthetic 10,000-note/10-year output, which lands a note in every month by chance (`hasGap: false`, measured directly on all three). The two new `smoke.mjs` checks read this from the live vault and report a skip rather than a false pass, so the gap-collapsing path itself is exercised only by the hand-built vault above, not by the committed suite — flagged in `invariants.md` as a fixture gap for a future `make-shape-vault.mjs`-style addition, not fixed here since it touches the shared, digest-invalidated fixture store rather than only this ticket.
