| 2026-08-25 | **CORRECTION to the row below, and the real cause: the resting-layout check sampled one frame early.** That row blamed contention and it was wrong. The check blocked three pushes with `dtan` **30.3 / 35.9 / 26.7** on the 10k vault; classifying it as frame-reading and moving it to the serial lane changed nothing, and running it **alone** with nothing else on the machine still failed **3 times in 6** (0, 26.4, 22.1, 0, 0, 25.6). **The shape of the numbers was the tell and was there from the first run: bimodal, exactly 0 or 22-27, never in between.** Noise scales; a discrete outcome does not -- so the question was binary, and the binary question is whether the sampler caught the final frame. It did not. The rAF loop snapped `last` only on iterations where `busy()` was still true, so when `busy()` flipped it stopped **without** snapping, leaving `last` one whole frame before the animation's last drawn frame. That frame is worth nothing on a vault drawing **120-127** frames per cascade and **22-27 units** on the 10k, which draws **43** because every frame re-plans the whole vault (github#19) -- against a 16-unit threshold, a coin flip on one fixture and invisible on the other two. Snapping once more on the transition (still before the final assignment, which lands after `busy()` clears -- the reason the 320ms beat exists) gives **dtan 0 on all three vaults across 18 consecutive runs**, and `dr` on the 815-note vault went 0.1-0.2 -> 0 as well, which is the same off-by-one showing up small. **The page was right the whole time**; the check was measuring its own sampling. The frame-reading classification is kept as precautionary, not as the fix. |
| 2026-08-25 | **The most frame-dependent check in the suite was not in the frame-sensitive list, so it was measuring the harness.** *the last frame of a cascade is the resting layout* blocked a push twice with `dtan` **30.3** and then **35.9** against a 16 threshold -- a tenth of a 160-unit row -- on the 10k vault and only there, while passing **every** time it was run with `--only` and passing **three consecutive full runs**. Same code, same vault, same command: values from 0 to 36. That spread is contention, and the number GROWING between the two blocked runs is the tell. The mechanism is in the check's own method: it runs a `requestAnimationFrame` loop snapshotting every frame until `busy()` clears, then compares the last drawn frame against rest 320ms later. `JOBS` defaults to **4**, so a sharded check runs beside three other Chromes; rAF then drops frames, and the ones it drops are at the END of the cascade, where a 10k vault is slowest because every frame re-plans the whole vault (github#19, 14fps). `last` is therefore sampled further back from the true final frame and its distance to rest grows -- so the check reports the harness's frame pacing rather than the handover it exists to measure. Added to `FRAME_READING`, which puts it in the serial lane: 45/45 on all three fixtures with it there. **Worth noting what this was NOT**, because two wrong theories were considered first: not the ribbon measurement (`measureRibbon` forces a layout, but `drawDateUI` is not on the per-frame path -- `afterRender` calls `heatDraw` only, and the intro's sweep calls `drawRibbon`, which reads the cached width), and not a real regression in the cascade. The file's own header already said "numbers from a contended run are weaker evidence"; the omission was that this check was never listed as one of the ones reading a frame. |
| 2026-08-25 | **The date strip had never resized, and the ResizeObserver watching it was innocent.** Reported as "the timeline does not rescale when I resize the window". `fitCanvas` pins an inline pixel width on the strip's canvas -- it has to, since the bitmap is sized in device pixels and the CSS box has to stay in CSS pixels -- and an inline width beats the stylesheet's `width:100%`. `ribbonW()` asked the **canvas** how wide it was, so it got back the width it had last been drawn at, for ever: the first measurement won and every later one confirmed it. Measured on the real vault by driving a headed Chrome through four window widths: strip **1168px in a 668px slot, 1168px in a 1568px slot, 1168px in a 428px slot**, with the last year chip parked at 1101px in all four -- 673px off the right edge of a 428px band. On a page whose very first call ran before layout the strip came up at the **600px fallback in a 1284px band** and stayed there. The observer was wired, was firing, and redrew at the same stale number every time, which is exactly why this read as "there is no resize handling" when the handling was the one part that worked. `measureRibbon()` drops the inline width, reads the box the stylesheet gives, and **puts the inline width back**, so measurement has no side effect -- the first version restored it only on failure, which left the canvas stylesheet-sized after any observation the guard short-circuited: the same number by luck, and a stretched bitmap plus a sub-pixel box mismatch the moment a slot came out fractional. It runs from the draw path and the observer only, never from a pointermove, which reads the cached `ribW`. The observer guards on the width having actually changed -- that both saves a redraw per band reflow and breaks the feedback loop, since drawing writes the canvas size and the canvas is inside the observed element -- and a `window.resize` fallback was added for the no-ResizeObserver case, which had no redraw path at all. After, on all three fixtures: strip tracks slot to within **1px** at 62%, 128% and 100% of the lane's width, inline width equals the box at every step, and the year chips move (`468/468`, `1302/1302`, `948/948`). **Not reproducible in a hidden tab**: ResizeObserver notifications are delivered as part of the rendering steps, so a non-compositing pane reports `document.hidden === true` and never fires them -- the first attempt to measure this produced a page that appeared not to have the bug and did. |
| 2026-08-25 | **The intro is the right-hand scrubber travelling, and the mapping had to go through the rank.** The sidebar's Timeline block was deleted (a rank slider, Play, All) because the ribbon under the band scrubs the same history, in the place that draws it -- and that left the intro with nothing to say what it was doing. `sweepTo` now moves the strip's `to` handle, with the same `#vg-rtip` tooltip a real drag shows. **A preview, not a state write:** `state.from`/`state.to` stay null throughout, verified per frame, because writing them would put a hard date cap in `timeFactor` on top of the rank ramp the cascade is already animating -- and a range change goes `applyRange` -> `cascade` -> `stopPlay`, so the second reveal would cancel the first. `brushEnds()` checks a live drag BEFORE the sweep, so a hand on the handle wins. **The mapping was the real decision, and the obvious one is wrong.** Interpolating `dateSpan.lo -> dateSpan.hi` linearly desynchronises by construction: the reveal is ordered by rank, so at half the run the handle would read 2020 while every note from 2026 was already lit. Going through `tlDateMs[pr * tlMax]` makes the handle mean "everything left of here is on screen", the same thing it means under a hand. Measured, sampling the drawn handle and the lit count every 120ms on the real vault: handle **0.002 -> 0.698 of the strip in the first 240ms** (24 of 457 notes lit, ranks 1-24 spanning 2015 to 2023), 0.98 by 1.4s, then the last 2% of the strip over four seconds while 330 more notes arrive -- the handle crawls where the vault is dense and sprints where it is empty. That is this vault's own distribution and not a mapping artefact: the demo vault (1402 notes, 2 dense years) runs **0.030 -> 0.211 over the first second** and the 10k synthetic (10 even years) **0.010 -> 0.169**, both near-linear. The tooltip's date advances continuously throughout, so the apparent stall is not dead motion. Pinned by *the intro sweeps the range end across the strip*: 24-26 sweeping frames at `timeScale 0.25`, first sample 0.002-0.054, last **1.000**, **0** backwards steps, landing on `x1 === w`, state null throughout, handle labelled while sweeping and not after -- on all three fixtures. |

| 2026-08-24 | **Fixtures moved to a shared, self-invalidating store (github#20).** `gen()` regenerated a fixture only when its directory was missing, so every worktree kept whatever it generated whenever -- and the push gate's verdict depended on which directory the push ran from. Measured cost the day it was filed: the develop checkout held a **468-note** demo vault from before the generator fix, the feature worktree the **1,406-note** one from after, and two checks failed on fixture content while the code was innocent ("hovering '2021' haloed **0 of its 0** notes" -- a year that vault genuinely did not populate; a range change measuring **2766 units** of travel where the current fixture travels 144). The issue's own premise was half wrong and the research corrected it: the generators ARE seeded (mulberry32, `--seed 1`) -- the hidden input is `--end` defaulting to today, deliberately, so the 52-week heatmap window stays exercised. A fixture's identity is therefore (generator sources, args, generation day), and staleness has two modes: the generator changed, and the fixture AGED. Fixtures now live at `<main repo>/.fixtures/<name>-<digest8>` -- digest = sha256 over the CONTENTS of all three generators plus the args (content, not mtime: branch switches rewrite mtimes without changing a byte, and all three sources feed every digest because make-demo-vault delegates to make-test-vault) -- resolved through git's common dir so every worktree and the hook share one copy. A `.stamp.json` carries the generation day; older than **7 days** regenerates, so the today-relative checks never drift more than a week off the clock. Generation goes into a `.building-` scratch dir and is renamed into place only after the stamp is written, so an interrupted run can never be mistaken for a fixture; older digests of the same name are pruned. A leftover fixture directory in a checkout root is IGNORED with a one-line notice -- honouring it would rearm the exact incident -- and `--vault` stays the explicit override. |
| 2026-08-23 | **The gap that was too wide was the CHANNEL, and two rounds of shrinking other things proved it.** Channel over an ordinary step, measured on three vaults: **1.60, 1.72, 1.52** -- and almost none of it the seam. Taking the seam from 24 units to 12 moved that number from 1.60 to **1.60**; adding an excess on top of the existing margin made it **1.90**, because the margin plus each end note own radius already WAS the excess. The quantity to name is therefore the channel: what a boundary has over one note-to-note step, with half a pitch per side and nothing added as the zero point (two wedges each holding their end note half a step in from their own edge put those notes exactly one step apart, which is invisible). Scaled as one quantity by the rows falloff: **1.32 / 1.23 / 1.07** against 1.30 / 1.18 / 1.00 asked for. Verified neutral first -- at EXCESS_KEEP 0 it reproduces 1.60 / 1.72 / 1.52 exactly, so the new phrasing is the old geometry until the constant moves. The end margin also stopped ADDING each note radius and corrects for how it differs from a typical one instead: adding it bought edge symmetry at a cost of ~126 units of width at every boundary, and the difference buys the same symmetry for nothing on average. An ordinary step widened as a side effect, **150/143/156 -> 155/156/163** -- margin the wedges are not spending is room the notes get. |
| 2026-08-23 | **Dot size was fighting the camera, and no seam could have survived it.** Sigma's default `zoomToSizeRatioFunction` is `Math.sqrt`, so a node's drawn radius goes as `1/sqrt(ratio)` while its POSITION goes as `1/ratio` -- the two diverge on every zoom, and no choice of size holds a dot at a fixed fraction of the gap to its neighbour. Measured drawn diameter over row pitch: **0.76 at rest, 1.44 three notches in, 3.51 at the far end** -- dots swallowing their neighbours. Set to identity, so sizes follow the same law as positions, and the figures handed in are pinned to the lattice once rather than re-derived per frame: **0.787 / 0.786 / 0.786 across a 21x zoom range.** Two earlier attempts were measured with `getNodeDisplayData().size`, which is the reducer's output and NOT the drawn radius -- both reported a flat 0.786 while the screen showed dots touching. `renderer.scaleSize()` is the honest number; the label placer goes through it too, having been out by a factor of 20 zoomed in. |
| 2026-08-23 | **The size floor stopped tracking the lattice, and that was the dots overlapping.** `SIZE_FLOOR` 0.45 existed so dots could not vanish, calibrated against vaults whose rows land 22-30px apart; a 10,000-note disc fitted to the stage puts them **4.5px** apart, where the honest scale is 0.16 and the floor held it at 0.45 -- **2.8x too big for the lattice.** Measured: biggest dot **175 units of radius against a 160-unit row pitch**, 349 across, and the MEDIAN dot 192 across. Replaced by handling the two ends separately, because they want opposite things: the biggest dot is capped at a fraction of the pitch (or notes collide), the smallest is floored in PIXELS (or notes disappear), linear between, so the degree ordering shows wherever there is room and the range flattens rather than overflowing where there is not. Biggest diameter **349 -> 126** against a 160 pitch. A NODE_MAX pixel ceiling survived one round and had to go with it: it froze dots at 11px while the lattice kept growing, which read as swelling and then dwindling. |
| 2026-08-23 | **Every dot is sized against its own nearest neighbour, measured rather than assumed.** The row pitch is a constant; the along-row pitch is not, because a wedge's end margins come out of the same circumference its notes are spread across -- so a band carrying many wedges packs its rows tighter, and dots sized against the row pitch alone came out **126 units of diameter against a 120-unit step**. One figure for the whole disc was tried first and is worse: it has to be the tightest spot anywhere, and it shrank every dot for the sake of a handful, **0.79 -> 0.24 diameter over spacing disc-wide.** Per note now, keyed by id, taking the smaller of its two sides, and measured **across wedge boundaries as well as within them** -- those are the notes with the least room, and missing them left boundary dots overlapping by 27 units on the 1402-note vault (now +9). The 10k keeps a **-17 unit** worst case where the pixel floor wins over the cap; that is 0.48px on screen, against dots that would otherwise not be drawn at all. |
| 2026-08-23 | **The seam: a width rather than an angle, one width for every boundary, parallel-sided, measured to the dots' edges, and falling off as a band gets deeper.** Five changes, each from looking at it. (1) A degree is not a width -- it buys arc in proportion to radius, so the same 2 degrees was 75 units on a 454-note vault and 129 on a 1402-note one, which is where "the gaps are too big" came from. (2) Group boundaries take the SUB-wedge width, since the colours and the legend already say which is which and the wide version read as a missing slice. (3) Sizing it as a width and dividing by each note's own radius makes the channel's edges genuinely parallel: a point at radius r offset by w/2r sits at perpendicular distance w/2 for any r. Measured on the 1402-note vault, boundary arc at the inner row against the outer: **214 -> 205 units across a 4.37x change in radius**, where a constant angle would have given 4.37x. (4) The margin is measured to the note's EDGE rather than its centre: dot radius runs 4x from leaf to hub, so equal centre distances put edges visibly unequal distances from the boundary -- reported from a screenshot of the 12 o'clock axis with a hub on one side and a leaf on the other. (5) It falls off as `(5/rows)^1.5`, fitted to three judged points: half the base seam at 5 outer rows, a quarter at 9, none to speak of at 23. Final widths: **12, 4 and 1 graph units.** Third rule to stand where `gapScale`'s note-count ramp used to, and the first measured against the geometry rather than a proxy for it. |
| 2026-08-23 | **Date fields, year buttons, and a debug axis that earned its keep and then left.** The range readout became two `<input type="date">` fields bounded to the vault's span, grouped with All dates at the end of the heat row -- it had been loose in the middle, reading as a third unrelated thing, and the only way to set a range was to find a two-pixel handle at the end of eleven years of strip. The year labels became real buttons: they were painted on the canvas, which meant hit-testing a pixel band by hand and no keyboard, no focus ring, no hover state the browser could give us. | 2026-08-23 | **A 500-note vault and a 1500-note vault filtered down to 500 drew different discs, because the lattice spacing was a constant (github#13).** Reported as an impression -- "a 1500-note vault toggled down to 500 will render differently" -- and it was exactly right. `SP`, the lattice spacing that sets both the radial gap between rows and the tangential gap along them, was a hard 1. With the normalisation box pinned to the full-vault extent and the camera still, screen pixels per lattice unit is a constant, so **screen row pitch was a constant per vault**: measured 19.481px at every filter state of a 500-note vault and 12.064px at every state of a 1500-note one, while filtering 503 notes down to 62 moved the median dot 4.254px -> 4.208px and the hole from 32.8% to 43.9% of the disc. That last number is the exact bug `r0`'s own formula comment was written to prevent -- pinning `r0` into `geomLock` reintroduced it for every filtered view. **The solve is exact, not a fudge.** A lattice of spacing `s` holds `1/s^2` notes per unit area, so an annulus with its hole at `HOLE*R` holds `n = pi*R^2*(1-HOLE^2)/s^2`; setting that equal to the full-vault `R` gives `s = sqrt(n_full / n_visible)`, on the same uniform-density assumption the `r0` formula already makes. After: `pitchPx * sqrt(shown)` spread **2.85x -> 1.10x** (500-note) and 2.82x -> 1.09x (1500-note), and every state below the density cap is **exactly** 436.919 / 467.219 -- invariant rather than approximately so, with the whole residual in the capped step. Median dot 4.238px -> 10.854px; hole holds 0.304-0.328. `r0` needed no change at all: pinned at `HOLE * maxR` it holds the hole at a constant share once the disc keeps its radius. **Three things were got wrong on the way and are worth recording.** (1) A **correction pass** that fed `maxR` back and scaled `SP` until it landed on the locked extent made it *worse* -- spread 1.10x -> 1.15x -- because it drove `SP` back to 1 nearly everywhere. The reason contradicts the premise the work started from: **filtering barely moves the disc's radius.** `maxR` is the max over cells, so the deepest survivor still reaches the rim -- `reach` was 1.000 at 481, 465 and 382 of 503 notes showing, i.e. there was never any empty margin to reclaim. Filtering makes the disc *sparser* at a radius that hardly moves, and that radius is quantised in whole rows and already flush with the box, so 2.3% more spacing buys 7% more radius and no spacing exists between "no change" and "one row over". (2) **`HL_PUSH` was scaled by `SP` and should not be.** It is quoted in rows, so scaling looked right, but it was sized as a fraction of the *radius* ("0.9 rows on a ~13.3-row disc is 6.8%") and the radius is what the solve holds still -- it is the row count that falls. At the cap the disc is ~5 rows deep, so a scaled push would have been 18% of it and a highlighted note would have protruded off the stage. (3) The **cascade must be handed the spacing**, not left to derive one: deriving it from `planTotal` mid-animation samples alpha-weighted membership, which slides every frame, and the biggest single-frame radial step went **0 -> 94 units against a row of 160**. Fixed the way rows already were -- both endpoint packings built from binary presence, per-frame value interpolated between them on the same clock as `rowsAt` -- back to 0. **`fitRatio()`'s upper clamp came off 1**, which was correct only while the disc could exclusively shrink; a spread lattice puts the outermost row a few percent past the lock (measured 1.079) and that is disc, not margin, so fit was framing a filtered disc with its rim cut off. **Continuous on purpose.** The retired `REPACK_BELOW` mechanism switched plan basis at 55% and all three of its recorded failures were failures of the *threshold*; a smooth function of visible weight has no side to be on, and it keeps both plan invariants for free -- a departing note sits at weight 0 and adds nothing to `planTotal`, and the lean and padded plans each derive it from their own. Two new checks in `scripts/smoke.mjs` and a new `scripts/measure-density.mjs`, which is the instrument this was built with rather than an afterthought. |

One command to enable per clone: `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`. pre-push and not pre-commit because the suite builds the page and drives a real Chrome, ~30s: a fine price per push, an intolerable one per commit. `SKIP_SMOKE=1 git push` skips it deliberately; the hook does not mention `--no-verify`, which silently disables every other hook and is the habit that makes hooks worthless. | 2026-08-22 | **The highlight ramp is PER NOTE, not one global scalar.** Highlights are additive -- two groups can be lit at once, a subfolder can be lit inside an already-lit group, and a heatmap hover swaps one whole set for another -- so a single ramp would pop the second set in at whatever value the first had left it, and could not fade one set out while another fades in. `hl[id]` follows the pattern `alpha` already establishes. Everything about the treatment now keys on `hl[id]` rather than on `isHighlighted(id)` -- on the way out the predicate is already false while the ramp is still above zero, so keying the halo on it dropped the ring on the fade's first frame and left a shrinking bare dot. The ring is MIXED from the note's own colour instead of alpha-faded, so it emerges from the dot rather than ghosting over it and stays a solid hex, which is the only thing Sigma's colour parser is reliable about. Wall-clock and frame-clamped like every other animation here, so a stalled frame stretches it rather than leaping. Mixing is sRGB, not OKLab: this runs per node per frame and both endpoints are known, so a perceptual path buys nothing visible and costs two cbrt per channel. Two more found the same way, by measuring the output instead of reading the code: `gdigrab -i title=` is an exact match and Chrome's window is `<page> - Google Chrome` (captures a `GetWindowRect` region now, which also survives the page renaming itself as its completion signal), and `ffmpeg -i <file>` reports to stderr and exits non-zero, which PowerShell 5.1 turns into a `NativeCommandError` on a recording that had in fact worked. | 2026-08-22 | **Today is marked by an arrow in the right margin, on its ROW.** A ring alone could not do it: every ring on the band is `--today` and they differ only in weight, so today's was the weakest of three *and* disappeared entirely whenever the same cell was hovered or picked, since the three are one if/else chain. It points at the row rather than the column because the **column is a foregone conclusion**: `start` is `(cols - 1)` weeks before this Monday, so the trailing column is always the current week, and pointing at it says nothing. | 2026-08-22 | **Three encodings for the heatmap, two of them measured wrong before the third stuck.** *Averaging* each day's colours failed in both directions: mixing many hues in OKLab collapses toward grey, so the busiest day — being also the most mixed — rendered **duller** than a quiet single-colour day, 180 notes at OKLab L=0.713 against **L=0.781 for a 13-note day**, and with five quantile levels the count channel was flat at the top (every day from 8 notes up shared the last bucket) and overlapping at the bottom (1-note days reached L=0.47 while 2-note days started at 0.45). Worse, the mean is a colour no note has. *Sizing the square by the count* did work — strictly monotonic, **5.2px at one note to the full 13px at 180** — but a grid of squares that mostly do not touch stops reading as a calendar. Final: full squares, tiled one block per note in that note's exact colour. Verified **0 of 48 day-cells partially filled**. | 2026-08-22 | **Blocks inside a square are ordered by HUE, and the square is tiled exactly.** Folder order was the first key and it is the wrong one: the group palette is assigned in *name* order precisely so a folder keeps its colour, and name order is not hue order — measured on this palette consecutive folders run blue 264°, orange 42°, aqua 168°, so grouping by folder puts the three most distant hues on the wheel side by side. Hue angle, tie-broken by lightness (the axis the subfolder tints move along), makes each square its own small gradient. Verified on the busiest day: **180 blocks, hue-sorted 1° → 255°**. The tiling is `round(sqrt(n))` vertical strips each split into horizontal bands, which covers the square exactly at any `n` — a row-major grid leaves `ceil(sqrt(n))`'s last row part-empty, and a ragged corner reads as a different count. |

| 2026-08-22 | **The wedge-disable jump, actually fixed: the gaps that POSITION the wedges were the integer ones.** Two earlier attempts aimed at `share()` in `buildWedgePlan`, which sets the reference width the row counts are solved against -- not the rendered geometry. So a fading group kept its gaps reserved all the way down and then, on the single frame its last note reached zero opacity, `nG` went 9 -> 8 and `nSub` 9 -> 6: **2deg + 3 x 0.3deg = 2.9deg redistributed across every wedge in one frame**, with no animation. Each group now spends its gap in proportion to its opacity (`geom / seats`), each non-first cell its sub-gap likewise, and every *placed* increment carries the same weight as the reservation so the wedges still fill the circle exactly. Measured with a static opacity sweep (immune to the rAF throttling that made frame sampling useless): the seated plan at alpha 0 and the settled plan now agree to **0 degrees and 0 units** -- and the fade itself moves ~1.5deg per step, 132.9 -> 134.5 -> 136.1 -> 137.9 -> 139.0 -> 139.4. |
| 2026-08-22 | **The small jump when disabling a wedge: the gap count stepped on membership.** `share()` took the total gap as `gapFor(n) * n` with `n` an integer count of the groups present. A departing group is present while it fades and absent at rest, so `n` dropped by one at handover, the disc gained a whole 2-degree gap to redistribute, and every wedge's share moved with it. Found by measuring **screen pixels** on the six outermost notes outside the toggled folder, after position, size, label, colour and camera ratio had all measured clean: `1965ms graph=0.2` -> `1982ms graph=33.1 screen=6.0` -> `1999ms graph=0.1`, one isolated frame *after* the animation had converged. `n` is now continuous -- each group contributes `min(1, its weight)` -- so a leaving group's gap closes over its final note. Tail now `6.9 -> 0.6 -> 0.4 -> 0.3 -> 0.2 -> 0.1 -> 0`, no spike. **This is not two planners**: there is one planner and one basis, and the two membership sets are irreducibly different; what was wrong is that a zero-weight member cost something. `__vg.checkZeroWeightInvariance()` now asserts it directly and would have caught this on day one. |

The plan is now always built from what is visible, at all five call sites. Filtering and highlighting are keyed by path *prefix*, so one code path serves every depth: `visible()` walks a note's ancestors, and hiding a folder hides its subtree without knowing how deep it is. Deeper levels take no tint and cut no wedge (they inherit the parent swatch — the pie does not distinguish them, so the legend must not pretend it does) but get their own eye and highlight; only depth 1 is ever pushed radially, since a deeper folder is interleaved with its siblings at the same angles. The centre now stops using angle: a radial core in the palette's own **mean** is laid over the conic, solid to 9% and gone by 34% (small on purpose — past ~30% the arcs blend on their own, and a bigger core washes the hue variation out of the middle). Measured, `t` runs 1 · 0.97 · 0.90 · 0.79 · 0.65 · 0.50 · 0.35 · 0.22 · 0.10 · 0.03 · 0, peak per-step colour change is 1.65× the median (was ~2.6), the last five steps decelerate 33 · 31 · 26 · 17 · 7, and it lands exactly on the pure-inner reference. |

| 2026-08-21 | **Rows now continue across sub-wedge boundaries: notes are centred WITHIN their row.** The wide gap between sub-wedges was never a gap problem — it was the row *phase*. `u` was the fractional part of the continuous row coordinate, i.e. wherever the cumulative sum crossed an integer, so a note could land hard against a wedge edge with only the angular gap beyond it (measured: pairs 17–67 graph units apart where two dots need ~126). Neither easy fix worked: 2 degrees of gap only reaches 46 units, and re-measuring with the dot cap at 11/10/9/8 left the same pairs colliding, so it was never a size problem. `placeCell` now runs two passes — rows assigned as before, then each note positioned by its own half-share of its row's weight — which yields half-step margins at both ends for free. Then the pad itself was swept and cut from 70/0.22 to **35/0.10** — the first guess cleared collisions but pushed narrow cells 8 rows -> 11 (the sparse-spoke failure mode) and added 480 units of raggedness, where 35/0.10 clears them at the *baseline* 800-unit spread, i.e. for free. `SUB_GAP` 1 -> 0.6 degrees; with the inset doing the separating, even 0.3 measured clean, so 0.6 is legibility not geometry. | 2026-08-21 | **Sub-wedges no longer overlap: notes are held off their wedge edges.** The 1 degree gap could never have fixed this alone — `u` is the fraction across a row, so a row's first and last notes sit *on* the wedge edges, and the gap's arc at r=1334 is 23 graph units against the ~156 two max-size dots need (2 degrees would reach 46). Notes are now inset by an absolute arc expressed as a fraction of the cell's fixed reference width, capped at 0.22; the remap is affine, so the serpentine stays continuous across a row tick. Got the units wrong first — `base` is in layout units and `EDGE_PAD_ARC` in graph units, so without `* UNIT` every one of the 19 cells pinned to the cap and squeezing each wedge into 56% of its span traded a few edge collisions for 29 interior ones. Clicking the label used to hide the group, which is the wrong verb for "where is this?": seeing one folder meant hiding the other eight, reflowing the disc into a different picture. Highlight pushes a group out by `HL_PUSH` (0.9 rows) and rings its notes with `createNodeBorderProgram`; verified as a pure display offset, with every other group moving 0 / 0 units and the pushed group moving exactly 144 (0.9 × UNIT). 0.9 rows is sized against the ~6% of slack that already exists between the pinned box (1.02) and `fit()` (1.08), so the resting disc did not have to shrink to pay for it. Measured on 2026-08-21: **0 notes created today against 3 files touched.** The extractor now also emits `touched` (mtime), and "today" means created *or* edited today — which found the 2 notes actually worked on. It also gets the same treatment as a highlighted group now (pushed out and ringed) rather than only a colour swap, which was easy to lose among ten group hues. mtime is honest but not pure: bulk operations rewrite it wholesale (2026-08-19 shows 111 files), which is why both fields are kept. Also wired `touched` into the graph's node attributes — the field reached the data and stopped there, so the first build silently still matched nothing. |
| 2026-08-21 | **Dot sizes now scale with the row pitch on screen, fixing overlap on smaller displays.** Node sizes are screen pixels fixed at build, but the disc's pixel radius follows the stage, so the radius-11 cap — tuned against ~28px of pitch on a 1068x1270 stage — was calibrated against something that moves. On a 1080p screen (720px of stage) the pitch is 22.6px and 22px dots touch: measured 3 pairs, worst overlap 9.8px. `sizeScale = pitch / REF_PITCH`, capped at 1 and floored at 0.45, restores the tuned ratio exactly (max radius 8.87 at 22.6px pitch = 0.393, against the original 11/28 = 0.393); overlap fell to 2 pairs and 6.6px. Hooked to `afterRender`, not just the camera: `fit()` animates for 380ms, so measuring right after calling it read the pre-animation ratio and pinned the scale to its floor, drawing every dot at 4.95px. The 2 remaining collisions are sub-wedge boundary artifacts at equal radius, not size — `SUB_GAP` is angular and 1 degree is only ~3.3px of arc at r=1334. |
| 2026-08-21 | **A logo in the hub hole, and the same art as the favicon.** A neon brain — itself a node-link drawing — inlined as base64 at build time so the page stays self-contained. Three files because the build cannot make its own derivatives: `logo-source.png` (the 1254² original, kept here so it syncs), the committed `logo.png` / `favicon.png`, and `make-logo.ps1` to regenerate them, since `build-graph.mjs` is node-builtins-only and node has no image decoder. The source is cropped to its alpha bounding box first — the art fills only 73% × 67% of its frame, and base64 puts every byte in the HTML at 4/3 size, so cropping buys real quality per byte (224² would have cost 132 KB against 192²'s 99 KB). Sized from a measurement, not by eye: the hole is 180px across on a 1016px stage, the logo draws at 0.5 of that. Placed by projecting (0,0) and (r0·UNIT, 0) through the camera rather than by centring in the stage, because the hole's pixel radius scales with zoom. The `<img>` precedes `#graph` so the canvases paint over it, leaving room for hub notes to draw on top. Page 494 KB → 609 KB. |

On the `until = 0` frame that is almost nothing: measured, exactly **1** of 1419 edges has display data there, the rest being hidden at zero opacity. | 2026-08-21 | **Added a "curve edges away from hub" toggle.** Only 9% of links stay inside one folder, so most of the 1419 of them cross the disc -- and as straight chords they wash the whole interior grey. Curved, the dead centre clears completely and the haze concentrates into a swirl just outside the hub. It is a genuine trade rather than a strict win (the inner ring still sits in that swirl), which is why it is a switch and not a replacement. Bow magnitude is scaled by how close the chord would have passed to the centre, squared, so short intra-wedge links stay near-straight and only the long crossings sweep round; the sign is chosen per edge so the bow always points outward. Uses the `EdgeCurveProgram` already vendored in the Sigma bundle. |
| 2026-08-21 | **Fixed the timeline animation running in the wrong fill mode at random.** `fullRing` -- trade mode vs draw mode -- was written in exactly one place (inside `cascade()`) and read in another (inside the packer), and `timelineFrame()` never set it. So Play inherited whatever the last cascade had left behind: `false` after a fresh load, giving the pie drawn clockwise in wedges, and `true` after any legend toggle, giving the ring. Same button, two animations, and which one you got depended on whether you had touched a filter first. The timeline is a density animation by construction, so the mode is now a constant there. Detecting it from `present()` would have been actively wrong, not merely absent: the timeline empties the screen itself on its first frame (`until = 0`), so an honest check picks draw mode every time. |
| 2026-08-21 | **Refresh no longer navigates, and the intro is now the timeline.** Refresh used to `location.replace()` its own URL with a cache-busting `?t=`, which is unreliable on `file://` -- the one protocol this page runs on -- so the click could do nothing at all, which is how it was reported. It never bought anything either: the data is baked in, so a reload can only redisplay the same bytes unless `refresh-graph.ps1` has rewritten the file, and picking up new notes is that script's job anyway. Refresh now resets every filter in state *and* in the controls that show it, then replays the intro. First paint plays the same timeline growth instead of `clearAlpha()` + `cascade()` (which on an empty screen meant draw mode), so load, Refresh and Play are one animation rather than two that were easy to mistake for a bug. |

| 2026-08-20 | **The ring centre no longer moves: Sigma's `autoRescale` is pinned with `setCustomBBox`.** Sigma renormalises against the graph bounding box on every refresh, so a shrinking disc moved the graph origin 13px and zoomed 8.2% with the camera untouched -- every frame of a cascade. It scales about the centre, so the artifact grows with radius, which is why the inner ring looked silky and the outer ring jumpy. Origin drift is now 0px and scale drift exactly 1. Also: panning and rotation disabled, camera x/y pulled back on zoom so the disc is always centred on the stage (verified offset 0,0), Fit is a zoom reset, and `SPREAD_MIN` raised to 24 so a mid-sized folder's wedge does not close at ~6 degrees per frame. |
| 2026-08-20 | **The whole-disc snap at the end of a cascade is fixed: the resting plan is now weighted by visibility.** A cascade weights the packer by opacity, so a hidden folder shapes nothing; `settle()` then rebuilt the plan with every note at full weight, so the resting packing was the full-vault one and every survivor snapped back. It only showed on toggles that stay above `REPACK_BELOW` -- which is why `08` (221 of 442, crosses it) looked perfect while `04`, `05`, `03` and `02` jumped. Cascade-final vs settled layout: 0 units on every toggle. Penultimate-frame spikes 881 -> 213 and 1881 -> 323. Added `TIME_SCALE` / `__vg.timeScale` for slow-motion inspection, which is what isolated it. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Fixed two end-of-animation jumps unrelated to the packing.** The frame guard was a fixed timeout assuming 60fps, so on a page that renders slower it fired mid-animation and snapped the disc to its final layout; it is now a watchdog that only fires after 400ms with no frame, and the cascade skips re-indexing 442 nodes and 1409 edges every frame. And `RADIAL_EASE` (0.3) was pure lag now that the row coordinate is continuous -- the disc contracted 2133 -> 1731 across 48 frames and then 1731 -> 1653 in the last one; at 1 it follows exactly. The big toggle's last five frames now move 166, 165, 32, 58, 0. Still spiking on the penultimate frame: `05 - Weekly Reviews` at 881 and `03 - Resources` at 1881, not yet isolated. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Row count back on the live span, with placement as one continuous formula.** Locking it per folder was stable but degenerate -- isolating a 55-note folder drew a scattered cloud across 7 sparse rings instead of a ring. The row coordinate now comes from inverting the cumulative capacity (`base*x + SP*x^2/2`), so a fractional count slides instead of binning, and the serpentine keeps u continuous across a row tick. Replaces the two-grid blend that swung notes 1996 units in a frame. Resting disc: 0 off-lattice rows of 91; settle jump 0; big-group worst frame 359-380 (median 221-234). Still jumpier: an inner-ring group at 912 and `03 - Resources` (cells in both bands) at 1881. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Row capacity is now proportional to radius with no floor**, fixing the wonky columns that appeared once the reference width was locked: the floor bound on narrow cells' inner rows, so they held 1, 1, 1 then many. Notes per row now rise smoothly (the 81-note People cell: 8, 10, 10, 12, 12, 14, 15) and the irregularity measure for the affected cells fell from 2.42 to 1.13-1.66. Also unfloored in the row-count function, where the same clamp made a 19-note cell ask for 8 rows. Stability is unaffected -- a constant factor on capacity cancels in `cap/capSum`, so the note-to-row assignment never depended on the span. |
| 2026-08-20 | **A folder is packed from its own weight only.** The reference wedge width that row counts and capacities derive from was a live share of the band's total weight, coupling every cell to every other: hiding one folder re-packed all the rest, which was the source of the remaining jumps. It is now fixed at load per cell, so a folder's rows depend only on its own weight, while live angular spans still keep the circle full. Other folders' notes now shift radially by 0 on any toggle; worst single-frame movement 858 -> 119 (inner ring) and 1494 -> 237 (`03 - Resources`, the both-bands case). |
| 2026-08-20 | **Row count is walked by animation progress, not derived from the live weights.** Deriving it produced a fractional count at rest, so the resting disc was a smear of two grids one row apart, and the animation ended on a blended grid and then snapped to a crisp one. It now runs from the source packing's count to the destination's, hitting both exactly. Resting disc: 0 off-lattice rows of 98. Jump at settle: 0 on every toggle. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Removed every remaining step between weight and position**, which is what the jumps were. Row assignment is a continuous function of cumulative weight rather than a per-row quota; `u` is shared by weight rather than by count; rows serpentine so crossing a row boundary is a short move rather than the width of the wedge; and the row COUNT is fractional, with the placement interpolated between the two adjacent grids. Worst single-frame movement: 3191 -> 325 units closing the big group, 2970 -> 372 opening it, 856 -> 103 for an inner group. |
| 2026-08-20 | **The rings no longer affect each other's packing.** The hub radius was solved from the global weighted total and the outer band's base radius from the inner band's row count, so enabling or disabling anything in one ring re-packed the other. Both are now locked at load alongside the band split. Measured: hiding an inner group moves the outer band by 0, hiding an outer group moves the inner band by 0, and restoring is exact. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Density is now interpolated by weighting the packer with opacity**, replacing the blend of two finished packings (which pulled the rows apart) and the earlier attempt to switch the repack off (which left a filtered disc far too loose). Notes appear radially, a row at a time at the ring's current density, while their wedge widens circumferentially -- one calculation. **The two bands are also independent now:** which ring a group lives in is locked at load, so filtering can never make a group jump between rings; letting it follow the weights had flung a fading group into the inner ring across ~300 degrees. `REPACK_BELOW` back to 0.55. |
| 2026-08-20 | **`REPACK_BELOW` lowered 0.55 -> 0.3.** At 0.55, hiding the biggest group (~50% left) triggered a re-densification from 8 rows per cell to 5, moving every surviving note radially; since `r` and `u` are lerped independently across the two packings, the rows smeared rather than re-densifying. 221 notes over 8 rows is ~28 per row, so there was nothing to fix -- the spidery view the repack exists for was measured at 55 notes (12%). Toggling a big group is now purely angular: survivors' radial shift frame-steps at 0 throughout. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Vault-agnostic discovery and typing.** Removed the hardcoded folder prefixes: the crawl now walks the whole tree, reads the templates folder from `.obsidian/templates.json` (and Templater's config) and the daily-notes folder from `.obsidian/daily-notes.json`, and derives a note's type from the deepest meaningfully-named folder, skipping date buckets (`2026-08`, `2026-Q3`, `2026-W34`). The old map broke when the vault was renumbered -- `06` had become Monthly Reviews, so monthly reviews were typed `daily` and the `05 - Templates` skip matched nothing -- and would never have worked on another vault. <!-- path-check: ok --> |
| 2026-08-20 | **Overlap between the toggled wedge and its neighbour is fixed properly: cascades now interpolate the PLAN, not positions.** Blending two separately-valid full-circle allocations never yields a valid one in between (they disagree by mean 23.7 / max 138 degrees), so the survivors sat mid-arrangement and the wedge overlapped its neighbour by ~45 degrees. One blended plan per frame means angles are allocated once, in a single sweep, so overlap is structurally impossible. `buildWedgePlan` stores a cell key so cells can be matched across packings. Verified 0 intruding notes at every frame in both directions. |
| 2026-08-20 | **A closing wedge no longer lags the ring.** `ringsLayout` falls back to a note's current position when a plan gives it no seat, which hid "no seat" from the cascade -- departing notes were interpolating toward where they already were, so they never migrated radially while the ring re-densified around them (frame-stepped: ring 0.04 -> 0.90, wedge 0.000 throughout). It now takes a `strict` flag, and one-sided notes get a counterpart synthesised from the median radius ratio between the two packings (0.768 here). Ring and wedge now agree to 0.000 every frame. |
| 2026-08-20 | **The repack now rides along with the fade** instead of bracketing it. Crossing `REPACK_BELOW` moved 220 of 221 survivors radially (mean 371 units, max 655; the disc goes 2292 -> 1637 and back), and animating that separately gave three movements in a row -- reflow, fade, repack. The cascade now interpolates between the pre-toggle and post-toggle packings by overall progress, in polar space, so the other wedges resize across the whole time the big wedge arrives or leaves. Verified jump-free at both ends. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Big groups no longer vanish instead of closing.** Below `REPACK_BELOW` the plan is rebuilt from visible notes only, so hiding a group that crossed the threshold left its departing notes with no slot: nothing held their space and they faded at stale coordinates. `08 - Meeting Notes` (221 of 442 notes) hit this every time. A cascade now plans over the union of staying-and-leaving notes, tweens into that plan first if a toggle crosses the threshold, and tweens the re-densifying repack afterwards instead of snapping it. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Enabling is now the mirror of disabling.** An arriving note claimed a whole slot of geometry up front while rendering at zero width, so the ring carried a hole the size of the incoming group (~150 degrees for `08 - Meeting Notes`) that the dots popped into. Arrivals now ramp their geometry with their opacity, so the other wedges shrink at exactly the rate the new one grows: the circle stays full and only changes density. First paint still draws the pie clockwise from nothing, since there is no ring to trade against. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Wedges close instead of vanishing.** Hiding a group turns `visible()` false on the first frame, so allocating geometry from visible notes alone struck the cell out of the allocation instantly -- its neighbours snapped wider while its own notes were still fading in place on top of them. Cells now hold room for anything still on screen, so a hidden wedge's share decays with its notes: measured 152.5 -> 132.9 -> 42 -> 0 degrees over ~950ms, with no gap wider than 8.9 degrees opening in the ring at any point. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Refresh busts the cache** (`?t=` on the URL) -- a plain reload was being answered from cache, so a rebuilt snapshot still showed the old data. Added `refresh-graph.ps1`, which rebuilds and opens in one step, since the page itself cannot regenerate its data. |
| 2026-08-20 | **Reveal cascade.** Notes fade in one at a time, sweeping clockwise from 12 o'clock along the circumference, each wedge fanning open between its two neighbours to make room as they arrive; departures sweep the same way. Opacity is now the source of truth for rendering, replacing the instant `hidden` flip. |
